- **Mirror objects were never packed** (fixed): `dof/<name>.git` is created with `gc.auto 0`, so every commit's objects stayed loose for good, and each message rewrites its channel's tree whole: 1,240 messages in one channel were 6,210 loose objects, 43 MB (35 KB a message on 4 KB blocks); packed, 1.9 MB in 0.24 s. Every mirror commit now runs `gc --auto` in its DOF's chain with `gc.auto=1000`, `gc.autoDetach=false`, `gc.pruneExpire=never` (git's own threshold check; nothing pruned, every object is reachable from `main`); a failed pack is logged, the commit stands (`src/mirror/mirror.test.ts` "loose objects are packed"). ponytail: past `gc.autoPackLimit` (50 packs) git repacks the DOF whole, in a child but holding that DOF's next commit for its duration; a background repack when a mirror reaches GBs.
- **The WAL is checkpointed** (one connection, auto-checkpoint at 1,000 pages: a passive checkpoint by hand found it fully backfilled), but the file stayed at its high-water mark, and an FTS5 segment merge grows with the index: 4 MB at 1 min, 9.5 at 30, 12.8 at 65. `PRAGMA journal_size_limit = 8388608` (`db.ts › openDb`) truncates it back after a checkpoint (`src/db.test.ts`).
- **Retention: none, by design (v6's "logs kept forever"); the cost is measured instead.** No row is deleted: `tasks`, `replies`, `inbox`/`outbox`, decided `approvals` (requests expire to `expired` after 72 h, the row stays), `events` (their keys dedupe a replay or redelivery) are the audit trail and the Planner's input. Per closed chat: ~4.4 KB of `db.sqlite` (most of it the `logs` FTS rows) and ~8 KB under `dof/logs/`; 41,900 tasks were 185 MB and 329 MB. A Slack message: ~2.5 KB of DB (event, search row) plus its packed mirror objects. `tasks/<id>` is deleted on close (`tasks.close`, as v6/07 say; the soak asserts only the open parent and at most a run in flight remain), `clones/<id>` and the task's `schedules/<id>` with it; an empty run is discarded whole. ponytail: a `hortus prune --before <date>` (decided approvals' payloads, closed tasks' replies) when the disk asks.
- Left: an open task's `ctl/inbox/` keeps every message file until close (the soak's parent chat: 3,751 approval notices, 15 MB on 4 KB blocks; its sessions 1.8 MB), bounded by the task's life and budget; a project origin and `repos/` keep `gc.auto 0` (pushes under `receive.unpackLimit` stay loose: one task's push at a time, so slower than a mirror; the same `gc --auto` when they grow). `/tmp` is the host's and shared with every other process on this machine: no hortusd file there (`mkdtemp` only in tests), its entry count drifted with other users' work.

## Review round 7 — fresh eyes and docs

Read whole: `proxy/pipeline.ts`, `bin/hortus.ts` (with `proxy/internal.ts`, `server.ts`, `ws.ts` around them; `approvals.ts` and `gate.ts` under approval load above), looking for caps that disagree.

- **One request bound** (`REQUEST_BUFFER`, 1 MB): a Worker's `hortusd.internal` RPC body was buffered up to 16 MB and a `ws:text` client frame up to 16 MB, while the pipeline buffers 1 MB and streams the rest; MAX_INFLIGHT (256) of either were 4 GB of one Worker's memory. Both are 1 MB now (an RPC over it is a 413; a frame over it ends the relay). Upstream frames keep 16 MB (a big guild's `GUILD_CREATE`). Round 8 measured that ceiling and added the per-client byte budget (below).
- A push that hung up inside its ref commands left `peekCommands` unsettled (`readUpTo`'s round-5 `close` fix, applied to its sibling).
- Not changed: an RPC takes two turns (`server.ts` before the pipeline, `internal.ts` after its body): harmless, one queue.
- **Fresh install** (README tried on an empty `HORTUS_HOME`, its example `hortusd.json`, `NO_NET`): every page 200, `hortus ping|tasks|approvals|schedules|search`, `dof search` work. Fixed: the bins are `dist/bin/*.js`, so "put `dist/bin` on `PATH`" did not give `hortus`; the README links them. `node dist/bin/hortusd.js` prints `node:sqlite`'s warning; the README runs the bin through its shebang. A messenger without its credential said only "slack auth.test: HTTP 403" on Sources; it names the missing ones now ("no slack or slack-app credential").
- Real-service verification (Slack, Discord, Telegram, GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket) is still pending the owner's credentials.
- Verified on the merged tree: default suite 3 × (448 pass, 0 fail, 18 skipped); `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1` 449/0; `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1` 452/0; the image rebuilt, then `vm.test.js` (e2e and runtime) 14/0 without NET and 10/0 with it. The first merged run had 1–2 failures a run: `ws.ts` imported `REQUEST_BUFFER` from `pipeline.ts` across their import cycle (a TDZ error whenever `pipeline.ts` loaded first, `projects.test.ts`) and a gateway test asserted the Slack ack before it crossed the socket; both fixed.

## Review round 8 — crash recovery around the gate

SIGKILL (`HORTUS_TEST_CRASH_AT`, `src/e2e/crash.test.ts`) at each new piece of round 7's gate state, then a restart, asserting: no task waits forever, no decision applied twice, the card (`task.get`, `pendingFor`) shows what the Approvals page shows, no entry asked again (so no second DM).

- **A decision and its messages commit together** (`gate.ts › decide`, `atomic`): the status `UPDATE`, the task grant row, `addToScope` and every requester's inbox write ran as separate autocommits, so a crash between them left the entry approved (sometimes without the task grant the owner chose) and its tasks never told: an unattended task waited for good, a durable grant's `payload.waiting` tasks all of them. Now one `SAVEPOINT` (it nests inside `submit.ts`'s transaction); a crash rolls back to pending, the owner decides again (their click got no answer). An inbox file renamed before the crash stays (the seq is skipped, `inboxWrite` counts files): at worst a stale "retry" the Worker meets as a 403 pending. Crash point `gate.decide.before-inbox`.
- **Expiry likewise** (`gate.ts › expire`), and it runs once at start after every module is up (`hortusd.ts`, before `started`): an entry that passed 72 h while hortusd was down is told at once rather than a minute later. Crash point `gate.expire.before-inbox`.
- **Close**: its pending requests were expired after `publishLog`'s await, so a crash there left them before the owner for a closed task, and the log unpublished and `tasks/<id>` behind for good. The expiry now runs in the same turn as the status; the rest is `tasks.ts › finishClose`, which recovery runs again for a closed task whose `tasks/<id>` still exists. Crash point `tasks.close.after-status`.
- Fine as they were: `payload.waiting` is written synchronously with the reuse, so it survives any crash and the decision reaches every task on it; a tainted request never awaits (taint is checked, the entry inserted and the DM sent in one turn); an untainted request killed during its judge call leaves nothing and the Worker, reaped and resumed, asks again; a judge `allowed` row whose forward died is not reused (a fresh judge call, no double send); "already decided" from a second caller is the conditional `UPDATE`.
- Not changed: the owner DM is fire-and-forget (`coalesce`, in memory): a crash within a minute of a burst loses its "N more pending" line, never resends one; the Approvals page and the nav badge still show every entry. A closed task's card can still list a durable grant it met (the entry is shared and still the owner's to decide): nit.

## Review round 8 — mirror view scope

Probed round 7's `scope.dofs` on all three paths (host Landlock profile, the VM's per-task view, `dof.search` by a Worker).

- **A DOF removed or renamed in the config while a task runs still taints it** (fixed, `db.ts › taintOf › mounted`): the profile and the view are built at spawn, so the old `dof/<name>` stays readable to the running task, but `readable()` intersects the scope with the current config, so the DOF dropped out of the taint check and the task's gated requests went back to the judge. Now the DOFs on disk the config no longer names count too (only those the scope named; every one for an unset scope), each as `can read <name>, which is no longer configured` when it holds any indexed file (no trust rule is left to judge its authors by). `src/mirror/mirror.test.ts` "scope.dofs". A stale directory taints chats (unset scope) until the owner deletes it; chats are already tainted by `logs` in practice.
- **DOF names are validated in the config** (fixed, `config.ts › DOF_NAME`): any string was a key. `..` made `paths.dof("..")` HORTUS_HOME, so the host profile's carve-out for a task scoped to it granted `ro` on `vault.json` (the Planner's check only asks that the name be configured); `x.git` is DOF `x`'s bare repo; `logs`/`transforms` collide with hortusd's own DOFs (`transforms` never taints). Now: letters, digits, `. _ -`, no `..`, no `.git` suffix, not `logs` or `transforms` (`src/config.test.ts`). Owner-written, so minor, but the consequence was the vault.
- Fine as built, now pinned or checked by hand: a link inside a readable DOF pointing at another DOF (`../../slack2/…`) is EACCES on the host (Landlock checks the target's own path) and dangles in the view (the guest resolves it inside `/dof`, where only the scope's DOFs are bound); a DOF whose name is a prefix of another's (`slack`/`slack2`) is not readable (`sandbox.test.ts`); the mirror writer never creates links (`writeAtomic` writes regular files; `unsafe` refuses `..`, `.git`, absolute paths); the Planner's `scope.dofs` entries are checked against the DOF names (`/`, `..`, `.git`, `""`: rejected; 10,000 copies of one name: `readable` filters the name list, it never builds a path from the input); the view's bind mounts follow a sync during the run (files replaced by rename, new channels and threads appear, writes are EROFS; checked with the real wrapper and a probing virtiofsd), and never appear in the host's mountinfo; a hortusd SIGKILL under a running VM: the wrapper has exec'd into `virtiofsd`, so the orphan scan finds it by argv, and its mount namespace dies with it (VM e2e: no QEMU, virtiofsd or namespace left after the crash tests); `dof.search`'s `only` is ANDed in SQL, so neither hits nor snippets come from another DOF, and `--in` an out-of-scope DOF is `not_found`.
- Not changed (nits): the view's worktrees carry their `.git` file naming the host's `dof/<name>.git` path (git does not work on `/dof` in the guest; host Workers have the repos); `--in` distinguishes "not in this task's scope.dofs" from "no DOF named" (it reveals that a DOF name exists); a DOF directory that does not exist yet when a VM starts is not in its view until the next start (as for a DOF added to the config).
- Not tested in a VM: v6 step 5's "a VM task clones the origin, pushes, and merges through the same gate" needs the tap (`HORTUS_TEST_NET=1`); merges are tested with host Workers (the same proxy git route and pipeline).

## Review round 8 — memory ceiling and the restart loop

- **Request bodies per Worker** (measured, `src/e2e/dos.test.ts` "20 Workers each holding 64 request bodies open"; the `bodies` attack in `src/test/fakes/hostile.ts` writes just under 1 MB of a chunked body and never ends it, half RPCs to `hortusd.internal`, half through the pipeline to a buffering host): round 7's 1 MB bound times `MAX_INFLIGHT` was **+239 MB of hortusd RSS for one Worker with 256 bodies open and +4.98 GB for 20 Workers** (5,120 bodies; responses are bounded by `BUFFERING_ALL`, request bodies were not). Fixed with a per-client byte budget, `BODY_BUDGET` = 16 MB (`server.ts › admit`): a request is charged its `content-length` up to 1 MB (a longer one streams, uncharged), a chunked body and a WebSocket upgrade (its 1 MB client-frame bound) 1 MB, until it closes; over the budget a 429 with `retry-after: 1`. After: +48 MB for one Worker, +293 MB for 20 at 256 each (+133 MB at 64 each in the suite); the ceiling is 16 MB per Worker of request bodies (20 Workers: 320 MB) on top of round 6's response bound. ponytail: charged by the declared size, not bytes read; a Worker with more than 16 chunked uploads or WebSockets open at once gets 429s (none does today: one model call at a time, RPC bodies carry a length).
- **The restart loop** (round 7's soak saw a task restart about once a second): not the dispatcher's requeue, the hours tick or recovery. A start that fails (`worker.provider` naming no vault credential: `providerSecret` throws in `tasks.prepare`) or a Worker that crashes waits with `tasks.error`, as v7 says, but `tasks.inboxWrite` re-queued every `waiting` task on any inbox message, so each approval decided (`from: approval`; the soak decides one a second), Planner note, merge or schedule message re-ran the failure: one failed start and one error line on the card per message (reproduced on a real daemon: 5 notes, 5 more failed starts). A Worker whose model calls fail ends with a `result` "failed: …", which left `error` null, so the same messages re-ran its four model calls. v6 has no restart cap (Recovery only re-queues orphans); v7's rule "no restart without a new inbox message" was the cap and counted any message. Now: while `error` is set only the owner's message (`task.send`) or note (`task.message`, the card's form) re-queues a waiting task; the rest wait in its inbox for that start. A `result` "failed: …" sets `error` too. The card says "· waiting: a message from you starts it again" (`src/runtime/dispatcher.test.ts` "a task that cannot start fails once", `src/e2e/step3.test.ts` "gives up once"). Not changed: fixing the credential in Settings does not restart the task by itself; the owner sends the message.
- Noted, not changed (nit): a Worker hanging up mid-body logs `pipeline failed: aborted` at error level per request (5,120 lines in the attack above).

## Review round 8 — a day on a real daemon

`src/e2e/day.test.ts` (default suite, ~65 s, most of it the coalesced DM's minute): round 7's day, but every step real: a hortusd (`HORTUS_NO_NET=1`, user scope) with fake Slack and GitHub, a fake judge endpoint behind `dev.upstreams` (`planner.provider` is an `openai` credential; the fake provider plugin stands in for the `openai` type, so the Planner runs on it and the judge calls the endpoint for real); 40 owner DMs → Planner batches → `task_create` with `scope.dofs` chosen by the scripted Planner from the text (25 `slack`, where a stranger wrote; 10 `github`, owner-only; 5 none); each Worker's curl from its loopback alias through the proxy: 2 posts and a consequential delete under `auto`, retried while pending; 10 chats the same; the owner decides as entries appear (console API, the first task grant through the CLI), the Approvals entry checked for the task-grant button.

| | round 7 in-process | round 8 real daemon, before | after |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| owner decisions | 76 (1 + 25 task grants + 50 deletes) | 101 (1 + 50 + 50) | 96 (1 + 45 + 50) |
| tainted tasks | 25 | 50 | 45 |
| judge calls | 50 | 0 | 10 |
| owner DMs | (76 entries) | 2 | 2 (`coalesce`: the first entry, then "95 more approvals pending") |
| Approvals page | — | — | 44 KB at the end, 49 KB at most (50 entries a page) |

- **A tainted task's result tainted the owner's new tasks** (fixed, `planner.ts › batch`): round 6 taints every task a batch creates when the batch carries a tainted task's result (the Planner's brief may restate it), and on a working day nearly every batch carries one (Workers finish all the time): the 15 tasks scoped to `github` or nothing were all `created from a batch with untrusted events`. Now, with events waiting, results of tainted tasks wait for the next batch, which takes them (and no events); a steady stream of events cannot hold them back for more than one batch. The cost is one more Planner run when both arrive in the same 2 s. Tasks created in a results batch are tainted as before. The card's reason names it: `created from a batch with tainted task <id>'s result`. ponytail: the deferral is in memory; after a restart the results are simply in the next batch.
- **Every connector DOF taints** (documented, not changed; round 9 changed it, see Review round 9 — the taint rule's blind spot): each channel or repo has a `channel.md` (id, name, topic or description, members) with no `user`, and round 7 counts an authorless file as untrusted, so a task scoped to any messenger or forge DOF is tainted from its first sync, even the owner's own DM or repo. The effective rule is: **a task with a non-empty `scope.dofs` (other than `transforms`) is owner-decided; `[]` is what keeps a task auto-judged.** The Planner's default and prompt already lead there. Leaving it: a channel topic or repo description is free text any member (or a repo admin) sets, and attributing it would mean each of six connectors recording who set it (Slack's `topic.creator`; nothing comparable for a repo description) — the upgrade if the owner wants forge-scoped tasks judged.
- **Chats taint** by `logs` (every DOF), as round 7 said; their 20 routine requests go to the owner (10 task grants).
- **`hortus approvals approve --grant <id>`** is the console's "Approve, and allow … for this task" (`--grant` only with `approve`; an entry that cannot take it is `invalid`). "Already decided" from two tabs and the CLI: the second tab's decide and `hortus approvals approve` both answer `invalid: already decided` (exit 1), the entry decided once.
- **A task grant on the real path**: each tainted task's second post passed without an entry (`byOwner` = 2 entries per tainted task: the first post, the delete), a later post from the same task passes, a later delete asks again.
- **The live Planner** (`src/agent/planner.live.test.ts`, `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1`: the real prompt and tools, DeepSeek v4.1 flash through ollama.com, verdict RPCs to a recording stand-in, a batch of 7 owner requests of which 2 need a DOF: summarize a thread, search past messages). Before the prompt fix, 3 runs: 0, 1, 2 tasks given a DOF they did not need (`slack` to "reply to bob in #general", `github` for an issue comment asking to fix a README typo); the 2 that need one always got `slack`. The prompt now says the brief carries the events and their refs (where to reply), and a DOF is for reading what the brief does not carry, never just to reply or post and never for code (`scope.repos`). After, 3 runs: 0, 0, 0 unneeded on the 6 unambiguous events (the issue comment got `github` once in 3; the test accepts either for it), both needed DOFs given every time.
