- **A VM task cloned an empty or stale origin** (fixed, `dispatcher.ts › start`): the origin is synced from the owner's directory at `project.set`, at a host task's start (`prepareClone`) and before a merge, but not before a VM task boots, and the guest clones it itself. A project configured in `hortusd.json` had an empty origin, so the VM's clone was empty, its push was a branch without `main`, and `merge()` was refused "not based on main"; with an older origin the clone was stale and the merge refused the same way once the owner had edited. The VM path syncs first, as `prepareClone` does (`src/e2e/vm.test.ts` "v6 step 5", `HORTUS_TEST_VM=1 HORTUS_TEST_NET=1`: clone, push `hortus/<id>`, merge approved and landed in the owner's directory; failed before the fix).
- **Done-when clauses with no test, now tested**: `hortus tasks <id>` prints the card with the Worker's notes and the summary, and a second task reads the first's `summary.md` from `logs` (`step3.test.ts` "close"); the remote listener decides an entry with the phone's cookie, 401 without (`remote.test.ts`); a host session cannot reach the remote listener, on loopback or on a `chat.allow` host (`net.test.ts`, `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1`).
- **Done-when clauses still without their own test** (round 9 wrote all three: `src/e2e/step3d.test.ts`, `step4b.test.ts`, `step6b.test.ts`; at round 8 the parts underneath were tested, recorded, not built): v6 3d "a chat opened from the phone edits a project in place" (the remote listener serves the same API as loopback; tested: login, cookie, a decision; a loopback chat editing in place is `step3live`); v6 4b "the step-4 flow runs on each" of Discord and Telegram (the connectors, transforms and the Planner wake are tested per messenger; the Planner-routed reply is Slack only in `step4.test.ts`); v6 6b "the step-6 flow runs on each" (`forges.test.ts` runs the gateway half over all three forges; the Planner-routed half is GitHub only in `step6.test.ts`). Real services and a real phone passkey stay pending credentials.
- **Round 7's 4 `step6` failures under load did not reproduce**: the default suite with `--test-concurrency=64` (twice the 32 CPUs), three runs on the merged tree: 457 pass, 0 fail each. Angle 4's run saw one `dos` "20 Workers" `ETIMEDOUT` on 8 of 1,280 searches while three other suites ran beside it, not reproduced alone or in this round's final runs.
- Verified on the merged tree: `pnpm lint` clean; default suite 3 × (477 tests: 457 pass, 0 fail, 20 skipped), and 3 × at `--test-concurrency=64` (457/0); `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1` 458/0; `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1` 462/0; the image rebuilt (the Planner prompt changed), then `vm.test.js` (e2e and runtime) 14/0 without NET and 11/0 with it.

## Review round 9 — the taint rule's blind spot

Round 8 documented that every connector DOF taints: `channel.md` has no author and an authorless file counted as untrusted, so a non-empty `scope.dofs` always meant the owner decides. Decision: **`<channel>/channel.md` does not taint** (`db.ts › channelMeta`, used by `untrustedDofs` and by `mirror/index.ts › untrustedHits`, so a search hit on one marks nothing either): it is hortusd's rendering of the service's channel or repo record, not a message anyone addressed to a Worker.

- Keyed on the path, not on "no author": other authorless files are messages (a Telegram channel post has no `from`, a Slack message with neither `user` nor `bot_id`), written by whoever could post, so they still taint. Only depth 2 counts: `D1/T/channel.md` is not a channel record (`mirror.test.ts` "scope.dofs").
- **The ceiling, accepted**: `channel.md` carries free text the service lets others set: a channel topic (any member, in Slack), a repo description (a repo admin), display names in `members`. A stranger who can set a topic reaches a scoped Worker's context without tainting it. The judge still sees the request, and consequential requests still ask. Upgrade: record who set it per connector (Slack's `topic.creator`; forges have nothing comparable), or render no free text.
- **What it does not change**: a messenger DOF hortus has replied in still taints: the bot's file is untrusted because it may quote what a tainted task read, and Slack's bot events are never `member`. So the rule pays for forge DOFs and messenger DOFs hortus only reads; a Slack DOF hortus replies in stays owner-decided. Lifting that would need each reply tied to the task that posted it (none is). A `members` DOF also trusts only authors with a live event from a member: one known only from a backfill taints until they post (fails closed; round 6's ponytail).
- Tested: `gate.test.ts` "a task scoped to a members-only channel": through the proxy, a task reading a `members` DOF with its `channel.md` and two members' messages is judged, one stranger's message and the next request goes to the owner naming them. `src/e2e/day.test.ts`, the real daemon, the 10 tasks scoped to the owner-only `github` DOF are judged now:

| `day.test.ts`, before → after | |
| --- | --- |
| owner decisions | 96 → 86 (1 + 35 task grants + 50 deletes) |
| judge calls | 10 → 30 |
| tainted tasks | 45 → 35 |
| owner DMs | 2 → 2 |

## Review round 9 — a chat from the phone

v6 3d's last done-when now runs on the real daemon (`src/e2e/step3d.test.ts`): through the TLS listener only, with the phone's cookie and `Host`, a chat opened on a host project edits the checkout in place (uncommitted, no clone, no merge: "the next sync carries its commits", git and non-git), the Chat view, card and Tasks show it, and the phone approves a Worker's merge (a chat's dirty checkout holds it with the reason and Retry). Then in Chromium at 390 px with the real `webauthn.js` and `app.js` (CDP virtual authenticator; the served chain is the leaf alone, so the test pins the leaf's SPKI). The server path held; what failed was reading it: the card timed each text delta as its own line (one word a line; a run is now one line, and rows that render empty no line); on a touch device Enter is a newline and Send sends (`pointer: coarse`: Shift+Enter does not exist there, so every message went at the first line break); the stream's end sat under the sticky composer, the reply's last lines hidden until the page was scrolled, and a chat's new approval landed below it (the page scrolls to the end with the stream and when an entry appears); a cursor (`Last-Event-ID`) replays every row after it, not the newest 1000, so a phone asleep through a long reply (a delta is a row) has no silent gap. ponytail: `/chat/<id>` and the card still keep the last 1000 and 200 rows on a fresh load. Noted, not changed: a link opened from another site (`owner.notify` in Slack on the web) is `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`, a bare 403 by v6's rule, and SameSite=Strict would drop the cookie anyway; a chat page holds 3 of HTTP/1.1's 6 connections per origin (SSE), so a few live tabs stall the rest.

## Review round 9 — step 6b end to end

`src/e2e/step6b.test.ts` (default suite, about 1 min) closes round 8's open v6 6b clause: step6.test.ts's flow on a real daemon (`HORTUS_NO_NET=1`, fake provider, scripted Planner) over fake GitLab and Bitbucket: the owner's comment → task → `--reference` clone, approved push, MR/PR by REST → refs; a follow-up routed, a stranger ignored; a review and failing CI resume the same `work/`; the merge event → `task_close`. Plus what step 6 has no test for: the owner's MR/PR → a project task → push to the local origin → approved `merge()` lands in the owner's working tree → CI on that MR/PR routed back by refs. GitLab runs as the harder shape: self-hosted, DOF `gle`, nested `g/sub/p`, bearer as the Worker prompt says.

- **Refs and repos were dropped for a DOF not named after its type** (fixed, `pipeline.ts › addRefs`): transforms name refs after their type (`gitlab:…`), connectors after the DOF (`gle:…`), so with no DOF `gitlab` the Worker's MR never reached `task_refs` and its review and CI never found the task (with one, the ref landed on the wrong DOF). A created repo was lost to the scope the same way. The prefix is now the DOF whose credential the request carried. Applies to every transform.
- **GitLab's pipeline event had no result** (fixed, `gitlab.ts › toEvent`): the summary was `pipeline g/sub/p by ci: `, so the Planner and the Worker could not tell failed from passed. It now ends in `object_attributes.status`.
- Fakes: REST writes now fire the hooks as the real services do (bot MR/PR and comment events reach the gateway, mirrored without a wake), REST-opened MRs/PRs get a real diff (`GitHost.diff`), Bitbucket has `requestChanges`.
- Not changed: Bitbucket's `commit_status.commit.hash` is what the connector and fake read; the published sample may carry only `links.commit.href` (from memory, not confirmed: the docs page could not be read). If so CI on Bitbucket finds no PR; check on the first real delivery. A Bitbucket event's `ts` ignores `changes_request.date` (nit). The Worker prompt has no forge clone or `hortus/<task>` guidance, unlike 17-github.md (the scripted Worker does not exercise it). A request carrying two credentials of one type takes the first DOF (none does). Fake MRs/PRs keep their opening sha, so a second push and CI on it is not covered. Real services stay pending credentials.

## Review round 9 — a second reader for round 8

Read whole: `vault/gate.ts`, `proxy/server.ts`, `planner/planner.ts › batch`, `runtime/tasks.ts › inboxWrite`, `runtime/dispatcher.ts › start, finish`.

- **The gate's SAVEPOINT holds.** A throw in it rolls back to the savepoint and releases it, and the caller's transaction goes on (`src/vault/gate.test.ts`, new, passed before any change). No await inside; `bus.emit` cannot throw into it; nothing it reaches runs `BEGIN`. The start-time `expire()` runs after every module is up, synchronously: a request before it sees a stale entry as pending and hears "expired" a moment later.
- **The owner's own Slack message did not start a failed task** (major, round 8's rule). `task_attach` writes `from: <thread ref>`, not `owner`, so "try again" in the thread (the card told them to) waited for good. `inboxWrite(id, msg, owner)`: `task_attach` passes `isOwner`. A stranger's event still waits.
- **Which messages start a failed task** (`tasks.ts › restarts`). Starts it: `task.send`, `task.message`, the owner's event through the Planner, an approval the owner **approved**, a **one-shot timer**. Waits: Planner notes, rejections, expiries, `merge`, `rebase`, `cookies`, strangers' events. Round 8 counted approvals and schedules among the restart loop; both are bounded. An approval is one owner click, and its message says "retry the request" while the Worker slept on (a login's Done and a merge's approval come through it too, so their `cookies` and `merge` lines need no rule). A one-shot row fires once and a failing task makes no new ones; recurring rows start their own `run`, never the inbox. Ceiling: one failed start per approval approved (soak: one a second while the owner approves that fast, each cheap or one session's model calls); count restarts per error if that bites.
- **The Planner could not see a failed task** (minor): its note was accepted and never read, and "## Open tasks" showed plain `waiting`. Now `— failed, only the owner starts it again: "<error line>"`, a JSON string; the prompt's Input section names it as data (Planner prompt changed).
- **Node's `requestTimeout` cut every upload at 5 minutes** (major): `server.on("clientError")` destroyed the socket, so a 50 MB pack to an upstream under 1.4 Mbit/s died. `requestTimeout = 0` on the proxy listeners; `MAX_INFLIGHT` and `BODY_BUDGET` bound what a held request costs, and its socket dies with its session. A raw connection flood was never bounded by `admit` (`headersTimeout` 60 s stays). The push itself passes: `src/e2e/projects.test.ts` pushes a real 50 MB pack (chunked, charged 1 MB, streamed to `git http-backend`).
- **`BODY_BUDGET` had a free path** (major): a `content-length` over 1 MB is uncharged because the pipeline streams it, but `/rpc/` buffers it in `readUpTo`, so 256 RPCs declaring 2 MB and holding just under 1 MB were round 8's +239 MB per Worker again. An RPC declaring more than 1 MB is a 413 before it is read.
- **What a Worker sees on 429**: `curl` the text and `retry-after: 1`. A model call: the SDK retries twice on `retry-after`, then the agent loop's four tries with backoff, then `failed: …` on the card. `git` prints only "HTTP 429"; the model retries. Enough at this ceiling: a Worker has to hold 16 bodies open itself. Role core never meets `admit` (`coreFetch` is in-process, the owner's own traffic): unbudgeted, rightly.
- **Deferral** (`planner.ts › batch`): no starvation. A deferred result waits one batch (`owed`); a restart loses `owed` and defers once more. Reordering is harmless: batch A may attach a follow-up to the task, and batch B then sees it `queued`/`running`, which the prompt says to leave open. But deferring beside a stranger's event kept nothing clean (the batch is tainted anyway) and doubled the batches against `daily_tokens` (minor): only all-trusted events defer now (`src/e2e/step4.test.ts`).
- **A failed sync at a task's start was only logged** (minor, host and VM): the Worker did the whole job on a stale main (or an empty origin) and met "not based on main" at the end. `origin.ts › startSync`: a status line on the card with the reason (`hortus sync --force` when rewritten); with no main in the origin the task does not start. A slow sync does not block the dispatcher (async `execFile`), only that task's VM slot, and syncs of one project run one at a time. A VM restarted after a crash syncs again and finds `out/work/clone` in place.
- **A restart or a close wrote a false error** (minor): killed sessions exit non-zero, so every running chat came back from a routine `SIGTERM` with `exit SIGKILL` on its card, which by round 8's rule also stopped approvals starting it. `finish` sets no error while stopping or closing.
- **An exhausted VM budget's card** said a note starts it again; the note boots a VM with a zero deadline. It says "raise its budget, then …".
- Decided, not changed: a crashed unattended task tells nobody (no outbox, so no Planner wake; `owner.notify`'s coalescing says "approvals"). The Tasks list shows the error. Upgrade: a failure line through `owner.notify`, coalesced apart, when a task that died unseen costs a day.
- Nits, not changed: a Worker's own `done("failed: …")` counts as giving up (a VM's exit code is QEMU's, so the prefix is all `finish` has); the start-time `expire()` has no try/catch (malformed `payload` only); `git` calls have no timeout, so a hung sync holds its slot and shutdown; STOP's 30 s kill finds no child during a sync or boot; `close` during a sync waits for the boot (`closing` is checked after it); a VM's sync time counts in `hours` but not its deadline; 16 open WebSockets refuse every body-carrying request, the model call included; the upgrade 429 has no body or `retry-after`; `owed` and `again` survive a stop and start in one process (tests only).

## Review round 9 — step 4b end to end

`src/e2e/step4b.test.ts` (default suite, ~100 s): v6 4b's "the step-4 flow runs on each", on one real daemon (`HORTUS_NO_NET=1`) with both connectors on the fakes through the proxy (Discord's gateway through `ws:text`), each test on each messenger: the owner's DM → batch → task → the Worker's curl through the proxy, approved → the reply in the chat, a reply to the owner's message; the owner's native reply to the bot's reply → `matches task` → attached → a second reply → the same again; a stranger in a group and in a DM to the bot (they do wake the Planner, as on Slack: quoted, `task_create` refused; only bots never wake), then in a task's thread (attached, `trusted=false`, the task tainted); a Discord thread channel and a Telegram forum topic; bot messages and the notify DM wake nothing. Closes round 8's "the Planner-routed reply is Slack only".

- **A reply to the bot's message on Telegram did not route** (fixed, `transforms/telegram`): the transform's ref was the replied-to message, but the bot's messages are never updates and `reply_to_message` carries no chain, so the connector puts an answer to the bot's message `B` on thread `B`: no ref matched, and the Planner saw a new message. The natural gesture on Telegram, by the owner and by a stranger. The transform now also gives the sent message's own ref. Such an answer still files under `<chat>/<B>/`, not under the root (the mirror has no `B`).
- **A DOF not named after its type lost every Worker ref** (fixed, `pipeline.ts › addRefs`): transforms write `telegram:…`, `addRefs` keeps refs of a configured DOF, so `dofs.tg` (or `work-slack`, a second workspace) dropped them all, silently; forge `repos` likewise. The credential is the DOF's name, so the prefix is rewritten to it. The e2e's Telegram DOF is `tg`. A Worker on a separate credential of that type (not a DOF's) keeps the type's prefix, and its refs reach a DOF only if one is named so.
- **The Worker prompt only knew `Authorization: Bearer`** (fixed, `prompt.ts`): Discord needs `Bot`, and Telegram's token is in the URL. It now says so and points at the transform. The image needs rebuilding (the prompt changed).
- `src/e2e/step4blive.test.ts` (`HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1`, no sudo): the real Worker prompt and a live model, no daemon, a `curl` on PATH standing in for the proxy: Telegram DM, Discord channel, Discord thread. All 3 passed in one run (36 s), with the new prompt only (no control run on the old one, so that the prompt fix was what mattered is not measured). A full step4live on these messengers needs `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1` (a live model's curl has no transparent proxy under NO_NET); it would be step4live with another fake, not added.
- The worker's negative control (the transform fix reverted) once took about 150 s to exit and once hit a 100 s timeout; rerun on the merged tree with the same revert, the two dependent tests failed and the file exited in 96 s, under its passing time, so nothing in the daemon's shutdown stalls on that path. The earlier slow exits were not reproduced and not explained.
- Noted, not fixed: the bot's messages (replies, notify DMs) are not in the Telegram mirror, as the API has no such update (15-planner's "mirrored" holds on Slack and Discord only); a held notify that fires during shutdown logs `owner.notify failed` once; `refs[0]` for a Discord thread is `discord:<parent>:<thread>`, so the Worker tells a thread from a message by the summary's channel (the live model did). The fakes are all this ran on: real Discord and Telegram stay pending credentials.

## Review round 9 — the final runs

- **The login view refused input from its first frame** (fixed, `login-view.ts › start`, found by the default suite's third run on the merged tree: `browser.test` failed 2 tests): the view entered `views` only after `Page.navigate` resolved, but the screencast already sent frames, so a click on the first frame was `the login view is closed: open the entry again`, for as long as the login page took to answer. It is registered before the screencast now; a failed start removes it. `browser.test.ts` "a slow login page" (`/slow`, 2 s; fails every run without the fix).
- **A batch could take an event that should never reach it** (fixed, `gateway.ts › eventsLoop`, found by `day.test` failing under the merged suite: all 15 judged tasks tainted, `created from a batch with untrusted events`): an event was inserted `consumed = 0` and set to -1 only after `mirror()` said it does not wake, so a batch running in between took a delete, a reaction or a join, and an authorless one tainted every task the batch created. Now every non-bot event is held at `consumed = -2` until `mirror()` answers (0 or -1); a failed mirror releases it to the Planner as before, and a start releases what a crash held (`gateway.test.ts`).
- **`database is locked` lost a mirror's index** (fixed, `fts.ts › index`, `planner.ts › batch`, `submit.ts`): a deferred `BEGIN` that reads then writes fails at once when another connection holds the write lock, without the busy timeout. Only the tests' own connections did that (`openDb` took `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` per poll); hortusd is one connection, but the owner's `sqlite3` could. All three are `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, and `migrate` takes no lock when up to date. ponytail: `gate.ts › atomic`'s outermost SAVEPOINT is still deferred; same trigger, only an outside writer.
- `github.test.ts` "CI wakes only with a ref" read its baseline before the review comment's wake had landed: it waits for the count to settle. `step4blive`'s Telegram test no longer requires `reply_to`: the model left it out in 1 run of 2, and in a DM a plain message reads the same and its own ref routes an answer.
- `pnpm format` also rewrites Markdown and `pnpm-lock.yaml` (and the README breaks `config.test`): format source files only, `biome format --write src`.
- **Test ports collided** (fixed, `test/until.ts › freePort`): the helper probed a random port and released it, so concurrent test files picked the same one; the suite's new daemons made it frequent. Seen as `timed out waiting for the console`, `the remote listener`, or `batch #1` with `EADDRINUSE` in `hortusd.log`. A port is now claimed by an atomic file in `$TMPDIR/hortus-ports`, removed at exit.
- Verified on the merged tree: `pnpm lint` clean; default suite 3 × (514 tests: 491 pass, 0 fail, 23 skipped; run with `KEEP=1` to hold logs for the flakes above); `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1` 492/0; `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1` 499/0; the image rebuilt (the Worker and Planner prompts changed), then `vm.test.js` (e2e and runtime) 14/0 without NET and 11/0 with it. Before the fixes above the same tree failed 1–5 tests a run.
- Still pending after round 9: real-service verification (Slack, Discord, Telegram, GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket) and a real phone passkey, on the owner's credentials. Bitbucket's `commit_status` payload is unconfirmed (the connector and the fake read `commit_status.commit.hash`; the real one may carry only `links.commit.href`, and then Bitbucket CI finds no PR): check on the first real delivery. (Settled in round 10: the documented shape is `links.commit.href`, the connector reads both.)


## Review round 10 — a second reader for round 9

Read whole: `gateway/gateway.ts`, `runtime/tasks.ts › restarts` and every `inboxWrite` caller, `proxy/server.ts`, `browser/login-view.ts`, `test/until.ts`; every `BEGIN`/`SAVEPOINT`/`COMMIT`.

- **A failed mirror never woke the Planner** (major). `eventsLoop`'s catch released the event to 0 and stopped there, and nothing polls: a Slack 5xx on the owner's message and it waited for the next event (older than -2; the hold's comment said it "goes to the Planner"). The catch wakes when it released something (`changes`: a bot's event stays -1, so the loop guard holds). A reaction whose mirror failed now costs a batch; it would have ridden in the next one anyway (`gateway.test`).
- **Sources said connected through every failed mirror** (minor). A connector whose `mirror()` always throws showed connected, a fresh last event and nothing else, while a Telegram mirror (no history to reconcile) silently lost every message. `Runner.error`, `mirror failed: …`, shown on Sources, cleared by the next success (`gateway.test`).
- **A stalled request was never reclaimed** (major, round 9's `requestTimeout = 0`). Measured with raw sockets, `requestTimeout` 0: headers unfinished or nothing sent, 60 s (`headersTimeout` stays); no ClientHello, 120 s (`handshakeTimeout`); a socket after a refused request (429, 413) with its body unsent, 6 s (`keepAliveTimeout` + buffer, the request no longer counted); **an admitted request with its body unfinished, never**. "Its socket dies with its session" holds for session addresses only (`unlisten` closes them): a VM on the shared address has no `unlisten`, and QEMU killed (budget, STOP's 30 s, close) mid-upload sends no FIN, so the socket, up to 1 MB of buffer and one `MAX_INFLIGHT` slot stayed until hortusd restarted. `server.timeout = CLIENT_IDLE.ms` (10 min, no bytes either way): a slow upload keeps moving, an upstream that silent is cut at `IDLE_MS` anyway, no Worker RPC is silent that long, and Node drops the listener from an upgraded socket (`pipeline.test`: a stalled body cut, an 800 ms upload past a 300 ms bound answered, a WebSocket idle past it still echoes). The bound is per socket idle: a Worker dripping a byte every 9 minutes holds its slot until the session ends, counted by `MAX_INFLIGHT`/`BODY_BUDGET`. An upstream stalling is `IDLE_MS` on either side, and `pipeline()` then destroys the client request, so that socket goes too.
- **A second tab during a login view's start never heard it fail** (minor, round 9's early registration). `open` returned the registered view, so a start that then failed reached only the first caller: a blank canvas. `open` looks at `opening` first; `login.input` reads `views`, so input from the first frame stands (`browser.test`).
- **A dead Chromium left its view open** (minor): every click failed and kept the view alive (`touch`), and reopening returned the dead view. `cdp.closed` closes it, `closed: the browser exited`, and the page says reload (`browser.test`).
- **The -2 hold holds otherwise.** Mirror throw, commit throw: released. Shutdown and reload: `stopOne` awaits the loop, so the in-flight event is released first. Crash: `createGateway` releases, and the Planner, later in the module order, wakes at start. A mirror cannot hang for good: each upstream call is bounded by `IDLE_MS`, 429 retries are 5 per call, and the loop stalls behind it for that long, as it always did (one DOF only; `gw.stop` too, then systemd's kill and the crash path). Nothing misreads -2: every reader is `consumed = 0`, `= n` or `MAX`; Sources counts by `created`, as it should a delivery; the webhook redelivery check and the member rule ignore `consumed`. Nothing in `dof` reads `consumed`.
- **`restarts` holds against every caller.** `owner`, `console`: the owner's RPCs. `approval`: `decide`'s only `by` in the code is `owner`, and the judge's allow is an inserted row that writes no inbox message; a durable grant starts each task on `requesters`, once per approval. `schedule:`: one-shots only. `planner` (untrusted), `merge`, `rebase`, `cookies`, a thread ref, `expired`, `rejected`: wait. Not changed, nits: a DOF named `schedule` makes its refs match `startsWith("schedule:")` (it would collide in `task_refs` too); `task_attach` takes any event id, so an injected Planner can attach an old event of the owner's to restart a failed task, at most once per call within the batch's budget (upgrade: only the calling batch's events); a Worker's `failed: …` with an undrained message re-queues once (exit 0, bounded by the messages, and the owner's own "try again" needs it).
- **`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`**: everywhere but `gate.ts › atomic`, left. Consistency there is not one line: it needs `db.isTransaction` (Node 22.16; `engines` says 22.15) and a COMMIT, and it changes nothing: both bodies write first, and a write-first deferred SAVEPOINT waited out another connection's lock (measured: 1 s, no `SQLITE_BUSY`). Noted on the function. No `await` inside any of them; nothing nests a `BEGIN`.
- **Login view, otherwise**: double open shares a start; `close` during a start waits for it, then finds the view; a double close releases once; the failure path clears the timer and releases once; frames after a close cannot arrive (`cdp.close` first). Nits, not changed: `close` during a start waits for `Page.navigate` (Reject on a stalled page, at most `IDLE_MS`); the failure path leaves `login-chrome` (empty; the next start or the task's close removes it); an `open` while a close is still releasing starts a second Chromium beside the first (real sessions share the cgroup key), reachable only by a tab reconnecting in those milliseconds; a view in `launch` at shutdown is not in `views` (its cgroup goes with the unit).
- **The port claim holds.** A killed file's claims stay and cost one draw each; 12,000 ports and one suite holds a few dozen, so exhausting them needs years of SIGKILLs without a reboot; the probe still refuses a port bound by anything else, and the claim then stays until exit, harmless. `TMPDIR` differs between files nowhere in the repo, and if it did claims only stop being shared. Nit: `reserve` swallows EACCES too, so a `/tmp/hortus-ports` owned by another user spins `freePort` forever; one owner, one machine.
## Review round 10 — the forge connectors against the services' documentation

Read against: GitHub's OpenAPI description (`github/rest-api-description`, its `x-webhooks` schemas walked field by field) and docs.github.com; GitLab's raw `doc/` (webhook_events, webhooks, merge_requests, merge_request_approvals, project_members, rest, authentication); Bitbucket's `swagger.v3.json`, the Event payloads page, Manage webhooks, the REST intro, the changelog and the end-of-life notice. Every fake below had the connector's misreading built in; each now serves the documented shape.

- **Bitbucket `commit_status` carries no commit object** (fixed; the round-9 doubt). The published sample and the `commitstatus` schema have `links.commit.href` only, so CI found no PR. `bitbucket.ts › shaOf` takes `commit.hash`, else the href's last segment; the fake sends the documented shape. Test `forges.test.ts` "a status finds its PR".
- **Bitbucket's cursor was a quoted string** (fixed). BBQL: `datetime` is "an unquoted ISO-8601 date time"; `updated_on > "…"` compares against a string, so every reconcile after the first was wrong or a 400. Now unquoted, `Z` dropped (offset optional, UTC assumed; `Z` is not in the grammar as published). The fake answers 400 otherwise.
- **`/user/permissions/repositories` is gone** (fixed). The cross-workspace APIs ended 2026-04-14, so a DOF without `repos` never connected. `/user/workspaces` then `/repositories/{w}`. It needs `account`, which only a workspace access token can have. Exact `repos` names now skip listing (`GET /repositories/w/r`): a repository access token sees one repository, and whether `role=member` lists it is unpublished.
- **Bitbucket's issue tracker API was removed** (changelog 2026-08-20; tolerated). History took 404 as "no tracker"; the status is unpublished, so 410 too. Test "history survives". The `issue:*` code and the fake's issues stay: the Event payloads page still documents them, and removing them is a v6 change for the owner. ponytail: delete `#id` Bitbucket threads when nobody can have one.
- **GitLab's individual approvals were lost** (fixed). `approved`/`unapproved` mean the MR's rules flipped; one user's act is `approval`/`unapproval`, so with two required approvers the first approval wrote no review and woke nothing. Both pairs handled. The fake sends `approval`. Test "one user's approval and its removal".
- **GitLab dedupe key** (fixed, a v6 deviation). `X-Gitlab-Event-UUID` is shared by a recursive chain of hooks, so distinct events could collapse; retries are documented on `webhook-id` = `Idempotency-Key`. Key: `webhook-id`, else `Idempotency-Key` (17.4), else the UUID. The fake sends a fresh UUID each delivery.
- **Trust followed the actor on GitLab and Bitbucket** (fixed). With `trust: members`, a member labelling or closing a stranger's issue made an event carrying the stranger's title `trusted`; GitHub's `author_association` already names the author. `member` is now `object_attributes.author_id` (a note's is its writer) and `comment.user ?? pullrequest.author ?? issue.reporter`. Test: the trust table, all three forges. A member's approval of a stranger's MR is untrusted too, as on GitHub's PR events; the owner as actor still trusts by `user`, on all three.
- **Merge and the other REST doors to a ref were not consequential** (fixed). v6: "hortus never merges", every push shows `old new refname`; `PUT …/pulls/*/merge`, GitHub's `contents`, `git/refs`, `merges`, `merge-upstream`, `update-branch`, GitLab's `merge`, `rebase`, `merge_trains`, `repository/**`, Bitbucket's `merge`, `src`, `refs/**` passed as plain in-scope writes, and the Worker's token can do all of them. Test: `writes` in the forge table.
- **GitHub redelivery ignored `repos`** (fixed). `webhook()` acknowledges and drops a repo outside the globs without a row, so the hourly check found it "missed" and mirrored it, waking the Planner. `request.payload` is also nullable in the schema and would have stopped the check. Test "the log's deliveries outside `repos`".
- **A check suite that never ran held `checks: pending`** (fixed). GitHub opens a suite per checks-writing App per push; `latest_check_runs_count: 0` ones are filtered in `checksOf`. The stale-`queued` behaviour is from memory, not a documented sentence; the field is documented and the filter harmless.
- Small: Bitbucket `ts` reads `changes_request.date`; GitLab `/approvals` has no `updated_at` on CE, so a review's `ts` was "now" and rewrote on every reconcile: `mr.updated_at`.
- Noted, not changed:
  - Bitbucket's member check and `/user` need `account`, which repository and project access tokens cannot hold. With v6's repository token every author reads "not a member" (safe; one failed call and log line per event; only `owner` is trusted) and the token's own events rest on `actor.type === "app_user"`, an Account subtype the docs do not tie to access tokens. `trust: members` needs a workspace access token. Check on the first real delivery.
  - GitLab `system` resets (`approvals_reset_on_push`): `user` is the pusher, so the approvers' `review-*` files stay. A mirror cannot list a thread (`ponytail:` at the site).
  - GitLab signing tokens (19.0, `webhook-signature`, recommended for new hooks): v6's `X-Gitlab-Token` still works and is what the connector checks. Set a secret token; a signing-token hook gets 401. Add a Standard Webhooks verify in the vault ops if the secret token goes.
  - GitLab `/changes` is deprecated for `/diffs` (removal planned with API v5); v6 names it and it works.
  - Merged-results pipelines have their own `sha`: the hook path is right (`merge_request.iid`), `checksOf(mr.sha)` on a later `put` finds none and drops `checks` until the next pipeline event. `head_pipeline` on the single-MR GET when it bites.
  - `work_item` events (tasks, incidents, …) and `tag_push` are acknowledged and ignored; a tag reaches the repo mirror at the next poll.
  - GitHub: `check_suite.pull_requests` is empty for forks (documented), so fork CI arrives by the poll only; commit-status CI (`status` event) is outside v6's table; `requested` is not sent to a read-only App, so `pending` comes from the poll. `POST /user/repos` is documented for user tokens; an installation token is likely refused (from memory), `POST /orgs/{org}/repos` is the working door. `{"repositories":[]}` for a Worker with no repos of the DOF: behaviour unpublished. Installation tokens are moving to `ghs_APPID_JWT`, longer than 40 characters; nothing here assumes a length.
  - Rate limits: 403/429 and `Retry-After` are errors like any other, logged and retried at the next 10-min pass; no backoff honoured. ponytail: read `retry-after` in `get` if a large install hits it.
  - `X-Request-UUID` across Bitbucket's retries is undocumented; if it changes, a retry is one duplicate event on an idempotent mirror.
  - `refs/pull/*/head` is fetched for every forge; it is GitHub's. GitLab's `refs/merge-requests/*/head` is not mirrored, so a fork's MR commits come only with the Worker's clone.

## Review round 10 — flows

A second push to a PR with CI running, CI for a superseded commit, the merge of what was reviewed, and the Worker's forge clone, on all three forges (`forges.test.ts`, "a second push moves the PR…", "a Worker following the prompt…"). The fakes grew `pushPR`/`pushMR` (GitHub `synchronize` with `before`/`after`, GitLab `update` with `oldrev`, Bitbucket `pullrequest:updated`), results on an old sha, and the merge endpoints with their real answers (GitHub, GitLab `409` on a `sha` that is not the head).

- **A result for a superseded commit set the PR's `checks` and woke the task** (fixed, `github.ts`, `gitlab.ts › mirror`): `check_suite` computed `checks` from the suite's own `head_sha`; a GitLab MR pipeline wrote `a.status`. A failure on A after a push of B showed `checks: failure` on B and resumed the Worker; a late success on A cleared a real failure on B. Now the connector reads the PR/MR live: `checks` is the head's, and only a result on a head wakes. Bitbucket and GitLab branch pipelines were already right (the lookup at receipt finds no PR for an old sha). GitLab's merged-results pipelines run on a merge commit, so "on the head" is also `head_pipeline.id` (test: "a merged-results pipeline").
- **A late `pull_request`/`pullrequest:updated` moved `head_sha` back** (fixed, `github.ts`, `bitbucket.ts › mirror`): both mirrored the payload while `diff.patch` was live, so a redelivery (the hourly check) or an out-of-order delivery showed an old head over a new diff until the next reconcile. They read the PR live, as GitLab did.
- **A CI event said nothing** (fixed, `toEvent` ×3): GitHub's summary ended `by github-actions[bot]: ` with no result; the Planner and the Worker's inbox got no outcome and none of the three named the commit. Now `failure on 0a1b2c3`.
- **The inbox's thread pointer named a path that does not exist** (fixed, `planner/tools.ts › task_attach`): `github/o/r/!3/` for the directory `o__r`. `dirOf` per part.
- **A Worker could merge its own PR without the owner** (fixed, the three manifests): v6's "the merge button is the approval, hortus never merges", but no manifest marked the merge call, so under `auto` the judge could allow it. `PUT …/pulls/*/merge`, `POST …/merges` (GitHub), `PUT …/merge_requests/*/merge`, `POST …/pullrequests/*/merge` are consequential. Like every consequential pattern this skips the project-scope check, so it asks, with the path shown, instead of a 403.
- **An approved merge landed whatever the head was** (fixed where the forge allows): the approval hash covers the body, so a `sha` in it pins the head: approved at C, head moved to D, the retry gets `409`, and D needs a new entry. `render()` shows `merge PR o/r!3 at 0a1b2c3`, or `NO sha, lands whatever the head is by then`; an unpinned call is shown, not refused (a transform's only refusal is a bare `502`). **Ceiling**: Bitbucket's merge call has no such field (its OpenAPI: `type`, `message`, `close_source_branch`, `merge_strategy`, `async`), so the entry says it cannot pin and an approved merge lands the head of that moment (the test pins this). Upgrade: read the head in the proxy just before the call; not worth it for a call the prompt forbids unasked.
- **The VM had no credential for GitLab or Bitbucket git** (fixed, `build-image.sh`): the image's `insteadOf` was GitHub-only, so `git clone https://gitlab.com/…` in a VM reached the forge with no placeholder. All three now; the test runs the script's snippet against `gitOf`. Needs an image rebuild. Still baked at build time (the script's ponytail).
- **A chat had no forge repos** (fixed, `tasks.ts › task.open`): `repos: []`, so every forge call in a chat was `403 project scope`, against v6's "chat tasks get every project". Now every mirrored project (`mirrored`, its ponytail says what that misses).
- **The prompt had no forge guidance** (fixed, `prompt.ts`, new `task.json.repo_urls`): the Worker was not told the URL (a `scope.repos` entry names a DOF, not a host; `gitOf` is the one table, shared with `sandboxEnv`), the read cache, the branch rule, what git's bare `403` on a push means, or not to merge. `--reference-if-able`, not v6's `--reference`: a mirror not fetched yet would fail the clone. A forge task reads the mirror only if the Planner gave it the DOF (`scope.dofs`, round 7), so the prompt's CI rule compares with the pushed head, not `head_sha`.
- Noted, not changed: a suite's summary is that suite's, so with two suites on a head a Worker can read `success` while `checks` is `failure`. GitLab `checks` is the latest pipeline on the sha, not the worst of a branch and an MR pipeline running together. `pending` results wake the Planner on GitLab and Bitbucket (cost only). Reviews stay `approved` in the mirror across a push unless the forge dismisses them. REST writes to `main` other than merges (`PUT contents`, `PATCH git/refs`) stop only at the forge's branch protection (v6 › Protect `main`). A push by someone else to a Worker's PR does not wake it, CI on the new head does.
## Review round 10 — failure notification and the chat page

What v6 says: Planner wake on "any unconsumed event or Worker `result` / `budget_exceeded` outbox message"; budget "Exceeded → outbox `budget_exceeded`, stop; the Planner decides"; a schedule's spent parent: "the Planner gets `budget_exceeded`". Recovery and the Chat section are silent on crashes; `owner.notify` is specified for approvals only, so a failure DM is this round's decision (round 9's "Upgrade").

Before, per case: Worker `result "failed: …"` and token `budget_exceeded` reached the Planner, no DM; the owner saw nothing unless the Planner's prompt led it to a Worker. Host crash, VM hours kill, boot timeout, failed start, spent parent: card only, no wake, no DM.

- **A failure now reaches the owner once** (major; `dispatcher.ts › finish`, `start`, `tasks.ts › failureRow`, `hooks.failed`, `hortusd.ts`): `error` of this exit, or a failed start, of a non-chat task → `task <id> failed|is over budget <url>/tasks` through `ownerDm`, and a Planner wake. `finish` runs once per exit and nothing rescans, so a restart cannot re-notify; a task restarted and failing again is a new `error`, so a new notice. Tests: `src/e2e/step4.test.ts` "failures reach the owner once" (fake Slack DMs counted: a burst of 5 is one DM and `4 more tasks failed`; a SIGKILL restart adds no DM and no batch; a failed restart adds one of each), `dispatcher.test.ts` "VM failures".
- **Text**: ids and fixed words only, as an approval's notice: an error is a Worker's output and the title may quote a stranger. `gate.coalesce` takes the "N more" text; failures have their own window, so at most one DM a minute each. In-memory like approvals': a crash inside the minute loses the "N more" line (Tasks still shows every error).
- **The Planner row**: a `result` (`failed: <error>`) or `budget_exceeded` row in `outbox`, seq below zero. The Worker's numbers stay its own (`finish` reads past `MAX(seq)`, which a negative row never moves: `dispatcher.test.ts`), and the batch's query, `consumed` and the tainted-result deferral apply unchanged (the deferral is not retested with a negative seq: same `SELECT`, keyed on the task). A Worker's own row is not doubled. Ceiling: on the card's Messages a negative seq sorts first.
- **The Planner's prompt** says a `failed: ` result is left for the owner, and no task to replace or report it: a Worker sent to post in the thread would fail with the same dead provider, its failure wake the Planner, and so on until `daily_tokens`. So the Planner does not post in the originating thread. Ceiling: the thread hears nothing; the owner's DM is the channel. Upgrade: a bounded notice from hortusd through the connector, if a stranger's thread needs one.
- **A Worker that died in a VM was a clean exit** (major; `vm.ts › startVm`, `agent/index.ts`): the guest's `ExecStopPost` reboots, so QEMU exits 0 whatever happened; an OOM or an uncaught throw (the default runtime for unattended tasks) left `waiting`, no error, or re-queued it with undrained inbox. `hortus-agent` writes `out/ended` (its exit code) last; none → code 1, `guest died: …`. Also settles round 9's nit for the Planner's VM. An image older than this reports every session so: rebuild. A hostile guest can only lie about itself. Test: "VM failures" (fake QEMU); not run on a real image here.
- **A QEMU gone during `attach` left its task `running` for good** (major, found by that test; `vm.ts › startVm`): the `exit`/`error` listeners were added after an `await`, so an instant exit (or a spawn `error`, a nextTick) went unheard, and the deadline's cleanup resolved nothing. Listeners now go on right after `spawn`. Test: "VM failures", `exit 0` guest (hung before).
- **A token `budget_exceeded` sets `error`** (minor; `finish`): without it any note or a rejected approval restarted a spent task, each a fresh `budget_exceeded`, batch and now DM. The card's "raise its budget" line now applies to it too. `tasks.budget` clears a `budget_exceeded…` error: a spent parent's guard kept the second overrun silent after a raise (`schedules.test.ts`).
- **A STOP killed after 30 s wrote an error** (minor; `finish`): non-zero, no result, so `exit SIGKILL` on the card, blocking approvals. STOP joins close and shutdown: no error, no notice (`step4.test.ts`).
- **A spent parent** (`runs.ts › onExit`) gets its `budget_exceeded` row as v6 says (the old comment "the Planner has nothing to decide" had no v7 decision), one DM, none when the run's own `budget_exceeded` told the owner already, nothing for a closed parent. A chat's parent too: its timer runs unattended. The row is not read for a chat (the batch query), the DM is.
- **Chats: no DM, no row.** The owner is at the keyboard and the stream shows the error (`reply`); v6 sends a chat's results to the console, never the Planner. Ceiling: a chat left open that dies an hour later says so only there and on Tasks.
- **A Planner batch that dies** (`dispatcher.ts › runPlanner`): DM `a Planner batch failed: its events may be unrouted`, no Planner row (a batch dying on its own failure would be fed it). Its events stay consumed, as decided; the DM is what tells the owner their message may be unanswered. Not on shutdown. A batch killed with hortusd says nothing (`step4.test.ts` "a Planner batch that dies").
- Not notified: recovery's closing of a `run` that never reached the model (a shutdown's effect), a VM task queued for a missing image (`vm.fallback_host: false`: not started, retried, the card says why, and `task_create` refuses it first). A Worker's budget or result landing during a shutdown: no DM from that `finish`; the row waits for the Planner and the re-queued Worker reports again.
- **Three SSE per chat page stalled the console** (major; `server.ts › events`, `app.js › connect`): measured in headless Chromium, HTTP/1.1, six per origin: two chats and a task card, and a `fetch` from the chat did not answer in 3 s (`app.test.ts`, fails on the old `server.ts` + `app.js`): Send hangs, on the phone's TLS listener too. A page now holds one: `/events/approvals,tasks,chat?task=…`, named events beside the unnamed chat rows; the single-name URLs still work. One each still fills six with six visible tabs, so a hidden tab closes its stream and resumes from the last seq shown, re-rendering on return. Ceiling: six visible windows; the login view's frames are a second stream while it is open. Upgrade: HTTP/2 on the TLS listener.
- **A fresh chat hid its start** (major; `pages/chat.ts › cutRows`): a text delta is a row, so one long reply fills the 1,000-row replay and the conversation's start was gone with nothing saying so. The page and the card's 200-row Stream now say how many lines are cut and link `session.md`, which has it all, open or closed (`console.test.ts`). Folding deltas not done: the live stream needs them as they come, and a write-side timer or a read-side regroup is more than the line buys. Upgrade: fold a finished reply's rows when a chat of 1,000 rows proves common.

Agent code and the Planner prompt changed: the image needs rebuilding (with an old image every VM session reports `guest died`).
## Review round 10 — the messengers against their public API docs

The fakes were written with the connectors, so a misread API passed every test. Read field by field against docs.slack.dev, the raw `discord/discord-api-docs` MDX and core.telegram.org/bots/api (Bot API 10.3, 2026-08-24). No real token exists: none of this has met a real service.

- **Discord never resumed** (major): READY's `resume_gateway_url` is a session-specific host, the fake answered with the one it was connected on. The proxy matched `gateway.discord.gg` exactly, so a resume host had no transform and no `ws:text` relay: RESUME went out carrying the placeholder, which the gateway can only refuse, and every reconnect (Discord asks for them routinely) became a fresh session that lost what replay would have given: edits, deletes, reactions. Manifest `hosts` now take `*.discord.gg` (`vault.ts › hostIn`, at `byHost`, step 8 and `frameOut`); `Hostname` still refuses `*` from a submission or the console, so only a shipped manifest can hold one. The docs say only that the URL is the session's own; `gateway-us-east1-b.discord.gg` is from memory and third-party fixtures, so the `discord.gg` suffix is unconfirmed until a real session (a host outside it falls back to a new session, below). Fake: `resumeUrl`. Test: `ws.test.ts`, "resumes on READY's session-specific host".
- **`coreSocket` hid every close for 30 s** (major, found by that test): `duplexPair` does not pass on a destroy on Node 22, so `ws` sat out its close timeout on each close, and a dropped upstream with no close frame was seen only by the heartbeat. Close codes reached the connector half a minute late and every resume started that late. Each side now destroys the other.
- **A reconnect that fails** (docs: "or the reconnect fails"): `session.url` was retried forever. After four failed connects the session is dropped and the cached URL identified on.
- **A thread member joining turned a thread into a channel** (major): every `THREAD_*`/`CHANNEL_*` dispatch went to `remember()`. `THREAD_MEMBERS_UPDATE` and `THREAD_MEMBER_UPDATE` have an `id` (the thread) and no `type` or `parent_id`, and the bot asks for `GUILD_MEMBERS`, so anyone joining replaced the cache entry and later messages filed under `<thread>/<message>/`, with refs that matched no task. Only the four channel-object events are remembered. `CHANNEL_PINS_UPDATE` stored an `undefined` key the same way.
- **One unreadable channel stopped Discord's whole reconcile** (major): `threads/archived/public` needs `READ_MESSAGE_HISTORY`, and `GET /guilds/{g}/channels` lists channels the bot cannot view until 2026-11-16 (the docs' own notice). The 403 threw out of `scopes()`, so `reconcileOnce` returned with no scope synced. Such a channel now drops out of that pass. Same loop: `has_more` is followed (`before` = the last `archive_timestamp`), and forum and media channels are asked too: their archived threads were never found.
- **Close codes the docs forbid retrying** (major): 4014 is the likely first run (Server Members or Message Content not enabled in the portal). It looped at the 60 s backoff, 1440 IDENTIFYs a day against the 1000 at which Discord resets the token, and Sources showed "no READY from the gateway": the throw overwrote the 4004 text and the rest had none. `FATAL` holds the reason per code; Sources shows it and the retry is hourly. Not never: nothing restarts a connector when the owner fixes the portal.
- **Deleted and cleared on Discord**: `MESSAGE_DELETE_BULK`, `MESSAGE_REACTION_REMOVE_ALL` and `_EMOJI` were dropped, so the mirror kept messages and reactions that were gone. A forward's text (`message_snapshots`, `content` empty) was an empty file; a command's answer (types 20, 23) was not mirrored.
- **`User-Agent`**: Discord's Reference requires `DiscordBot ($url, $versionNumber)` and says others may be blocked at Cloudflare. REST sent undici's. Heartbeat: the first beat is at `interval * jitter` as documented, it was a full interval.
- **Slack's `message_replied` wrote a phantom message and woke the Planner** (major): the docs' bug alert: over the Events API it has no `subtype`, the parent nested in `message`, the outer `ts` the event's. `MIRRORED.has(undefined)` took it for a new message: an empty file at `<ch>/<ts>/<ts>.md`, and a wake, on every thread reply. Fake: `replied()`.
- **Slack, smaller**: a reaction on a file has no `channel`/`ts` (`conversations.replies` failed, the event went to the Planner unmirrored); `me_message` is a person's text; `group_*` and `channel_unarchive` refresh `channel.md`. A half-open socket: Socket Mode here never writes except acks, and the global WebSocket has no ping, so after a suspend or a NAT drop `connected` stayed true and events stopped for good (the reconcile keeps files, not wakes). `hello`'s `approximate_connection_time` × 1.5 now ends the connection.
- **Telegram**: `edited_message` is also a live location moving, every few seconds with no text: an empty body over the file's name, a commit and a wake each. Same `blank()` guard as new messages. An edit dropped `reactions` (a `put` replaces the frontmatter) and could replace the media under a new `file_unique_id` without downloading it. `channel_post`/`edited_channel_post` were not asked for, so a bot in a channel saw nothing. `video_note` was no file.
- **Consequential patterns did not hold** (major, v6: "every delete is consequential"): Telegram takes GET and its method names are case-insensitive, so `GET …/deleteMessage?…` and `POST …/DELETEMESSAGE` were ordinary requests, and `deleteMessages` matched nothing; Discord's `POST …/messages/bulk-delete` likewise, Slack's by GET. Patterns start with `*` where the API takes any method (`glob`'s `*` matches the method), consequential matching ignores case (scope matching does not), and the lists grew by what removes data or takes the bot's updates: `delete*`, `banChatMember`, `setWebhook`, `logOut`, `bulk-delete`, `bans/*`, `bulk-ban`, `chat.deleteScheduledMessage`. Test: `ws.test.ts`, through the proxy as a Worker.

Noted, not changed:
- A reply to a Slack thread whose parent is older than the cursor, posted while the socket is down, is not in `conversations.history` and is never fetched (02's test list records it as intended). Closing it means polling known threads. A short outage is partly covered by Slack retrying unacked events; the docs do not say what it holds while no socket is open.
- Telegram: stickers, polls, contacts and locations are not mirrored (`blank`). An anonymous admin's `from` is the fake `GroupAnonymousBot`, `is_bot` true, so it never wakes: the safe side. `isMember` stays cached an hour after a `chat_member` change. A bot sees groups only with privacy mode off, and reactions and `chat_member` only as an administrator: no API tells the connector so.
- Discord: a forum's backfill writes no `channel.md` (the forum is not a scope; `CHANNEL_CREATE` does write one). Reactions read the first 100 users per emoji, and only normal ones. `GET /users/@me/guilds` is not paged past 200. `scopes()` and `history` cost one request per channel and thread every 10 minutes. Voice and stage text chat is mirrored live, with no backfill. `dm()` texts are one line, far under 2000 and 4096, so neither limit is handled.
- Slack: an edited message's unfurl wakes as an edit (`edited` is still set). For a reply's `ts`, the docs do not say whether `conversations.replies` returns the thread or the one message; `find` copes with both, up to one page.

## Review round 10 — timing races

The default suite at `--test-concurrency` 64 and 128 and at 64 beside `stress -c 32`, and under delay injection: a preload (no product change) that adds 0–30 ms to every `fs/promises` call and 0–90 ms to every `execFile` callback, in hortusd, the agents and the tests. Each failure was traced to a line.

- **hortusd could ignore SIGTERM** (major, `slack.ts › session`, `discord.ts › connect`; found by a run that stalled 90 min): `events()` checks `signal.aborted`, awaits `apps.connections.open` (`GET /gateway/bot`), and only then attaches the abort listener. An abort in that await had already fired, so nothing closed the socket and `stopOne` waited for it. Measured: SIGTERM 46 ms after `started`, exit 5,400 s later, which is `r10-msg`'s dead-socket timer, 1.5 × the hour; before it, whenever the service dropped the socket, and on Discord (heartbeat alive) never. The window opens at every connect and reconnect. Beyond a stop that needs systemd's SIGKILL, `gw.reload` awaits the same `stopOne`, so a Settings save changing that DOF never returned, with the old session running and no new one. Both return at once when the signal is already aborted (`slack.test`, `discord.test` "a stop during …": failed without the fix). `telegram.ts`, `forge.ts`, `pipeline.ts › bound` were right.
- **A hard stop deadline** (`hortusd.ts › shutdown`, `STOP_MS` = 60 s, under systemd's 90 s): the log names the module, exit 1; recovery at the next start reaps what is left. Proved by hand with a hanging `stop()` in `dist/`; no test hook was added.
- **Every stop with Telegram waited out the long poll** (major, `proxy/index.ts › coreFetch`; found by the bound below on its first run): `init.signal` was documented as not honoured, so `getUpdates` went on for up to `pollS` = 50 s after the abort: `systemctl stop`, and a Settings save changing that DOF (`gw.reload`, one DOF after another), hung that long (gateway stop measured at 34,972 ms), and the connector's own 65 s `AbortSignal.timeout` could never fire. `handle` already took a signal; `coreFetch` passes it. `pipeline.test` "coreFetch honours init.signal" (failed without it); `step4b` 93 → 63 s.
- **The e2e stops were unbounded** (`test/until.ts › stopped`): every helper awaited the exit with no bound, so that stall reported `ok` at 5,401,820 ms. 30 s, then SIGKILL, an error line and exit code 1 for the file (no throw: the rest of the `after` hook must still close its fakes, or the file never exits, which is what a first version did to six runs).
- **The transform deadline was wall time** (major, `transforms.ts › invoke`; `ws.test` under stress: `discord.request: InternalError: interrupted`, 502): a call descheduled for 100 ms was interrupted having done nothing, so with the CPUs full (a Worker's build) any request, a model call included, failed at random. Now the deadline needs both the clock and the process's CPU time past 100 ms: CPU alone would count V8's helper threads on a cold daemon, and the clock alone is the bug. A spin is still interrupted, by the same 100 ms of work. `transforms.test` "the transform deadline is CPU time" (a clock leap mocked; failed without the fix). `TESTS_MS` (500 ms of `transforms.submit`) stays wall time: at worst the owner resubmits.
- **`forges.test` wake counts** (test bug, proven; failed 2 of 3 under delay injection, once under stress, once for the forge reviewer): the webhook module's first chore pass, 10 s in, read back the deliveries the file drops on purpose and woke the Planner for GitHub issues during whichever forge's test had reached that moment (logged: `issues.opened o/r#7 … Dropped` in the gitlab run). The file runs without `redeliveries` (github.test covers it); `quiet()` waits for trailing wakes.
- **`step6b` "a failing CI result resumes" and round 7's `step6` failures** (test bug, proven): the last request captured ended `Write it now, as instructed.`, the previous turn's summary call, and held no CI line. `reqs().length > seen` counted that call as the resume. `step6`/`step6b` wait by content (`asked`). The CI line reached the Worker; the test read too early. Round 7's four failures are not on record, so tying them to this is the likely cause, not proven.
- **`soak` rss 19–21 MB/100 tasks under stress** (test artefact): the heap climbs 34 → 84 MB in the 1.5 min run, with no major GC finished under full CPUs; unstressed it saws between 32 and 47. 6 min under the same stress: heap −10 MB, rss 233 MB at the end (218 a minute before; that last step is not explained), against 229 MB unstressed. The short run's bound subtracts the heap; the long one keeps plain rss. Its 1 MB/100 bound does fail at 6 min under stress (1.49; 0.44 unstressed, passing): about the same rss over fewer tasks. Neither 6 min run settles native growth: the hour-long soak has not been rerun on this tree (round 7's: 0.02 MB/100 tasks).
- **`mirror.test` "48 MB of user turns" and `load.test` fds, under stress only** (test bounds): 1,025 ms against a 1,000 ms wall bound on what is a CPU cost (it bounds CPU now); `fds 111,110,118`, and `112,112,126` once in the NET run with idle CPUs: 22 of hortusd's sockets at rest are the test's own keep-alive connections to the console, and a burst adds to them for the 5 s they idle (no pipe or file count moved). The test counts fds less those connections, the low of a second: 12 runs flat at 90, where the raw count moved in 3 of 6; the 6 min soak's fds end −12.
- **`browser.test` login under stress** (test bug, by reading; once in 14 stressed runs, the next test failing behind it): it clicked at the first frame, which can be `about:blank` since round 9 starts the screencast before the navigation. An owner sees the blank page and waits. The form now tells the fake it has run (`/ready`).
, the `dos` "20 Workers" timeout**: not reproduced in any run of this round. The test gives up at 60 s and Linux's connect timeout is 127 s, so the cause stays unknown; no claim.

## Review round 10 — the final runs

- Tally on the way, default suite (a run is one full pass): before the merges at 64, 128 and 64 with stress, 3 each: 8 clean, one with 2 failures (`forges` wakes; the transform deadline). Merged, at 128: 3 clean; at 64 with stress: 3 runs, 4 failures in all (`soak` twice, `step6b` twice) and one 90 min stall. Fixed tree, at 64 with stress: 3 runs, 2 failures (`mirror.test`'s wall bound, `load` fds); delay injection: 3 clean (before the `forges` fix 2 of 3 failed the one test). Every failure is above.
- Then beside `stress`: run 1 clean, run 2 failed `browser.test` (above). After its fix: `browser.test` 6 of 6 under stress, the default suite 3 × 531/0 with `KEEP` unset and 3 × 531/0 at 64 beside `stress`. The NET, LLM and VM runs below are on the trees just before these two test-only changes.
- Verified on the final tree: `pnpm lint` clean; default suite 3 × (554 tests: 531 pass, 0 fail, 23 skipped), `KEEP` unset; `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1` 532/0 (a first run on the tree before `load.test`'s fd count failed that one test); `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1` 539/0; the image rebuilt (prompts, agent, `build-image.sh`), then `vm.test.js` (e2e and runtime) 14/0 without NET and 11/0 with it. LLM and VM ran on the tree before the `load.test` change, which touches that test only.
- Still pending after round 10, all on the owner's credentials or hardware: real-service verification of all six connectors and a real phone passkey. On the first real run check: Slack scopes and event subscriptions, `conversations.replies` on a reply's `ts`; Discord resume hosts outside `*.discord.gg` (they fall back to a new session, logged), archived listing on forum parents; Telegram privacy mode, `getChatMember` in a private chat; GitHub `{"repositories":[]}` minting, `POST /user/repos` with an installation token; Bitbucket: which token can read members (`account` scope), `X-Request-UUID` across retries, `app_user`, a quoted date's real answer. `README.md` › What each service needs is written from the docs, not from a real setup. An hour's soak has not run on this tree.


## Review round 11 — a second reader for round 10

Read whole: `pipeline.ts › prepare`, the six manifests, `transforms/*/transform.js › render`, `dispatcher.ts`, `recovery.ts`, `runs.ts › onExit`, `planner.ts › batch`, `console/server.ts › events`, `app.js › connect`, `slack.ts › session`, `proxy/index.ts`.

- **A consequential pattern missed another spelling of the same call** (major, security boundary; `pipeline.ts › routed`): the patterns deny, and matched the raw path, so `PUT …/pulls/1/m%65rge`, `…/merge/` and `/bot…/d%65leteMessage` were ordinary in-scope requests the `auto` judge could allow. RFC 3986 makes `%65` and `e` one path, and routers drop a trailing or doubled `/`. `routed` normalises those for this match only; `%2F` is tried both ways (one segment to GitLab's `projects/*`, two to a router that decodes first). A scope pattern allows, so a spelling it misses is refused already and it keeps the raw path. Test: `ws.test.ts`, through the proxy, with reads that stay ordinary.
- **Telegram takes its method from an argument** (major, security boundary; manifest): `Query.cpp` in `tdlib/telegram-bot-api`: `if (method_.empty()) method_ = arg("method")`, so `/bot<token>/?method=deleteMessage` deleted with no method in the path. `* api.telegram.org/bot*` makes a method-less path consequential. Same test.
- **Slack's deletes were a list of three** (minor, v6 "every delete is consequential"): `canvases.delete`, `reminders.delete`, `files.comments.delete` passed. One pattern, `*.delete*`.
- The wider patterns catch nothing ordinary: `*`-method lists name write methods only, a path that merely holds `delete` in a segment stays ordinary (tested). 
- **Held**: the merge `sha`: the approved entry is `used` on the pass, so a `409` ends it, the Worker reports, and a later merge is a new entry; nothing stuck on GitHub or GitLab, Bitbucket's entry says it cannot pin. `*.discord.gg`: `evildiscord.gg`, `discord.gg.evil.test`, a trailing dot and an IDN form match nothing (the suffix includes its dot, hosts are lowercased, a trailing dot fails `endsWith`); `Hostname` refuses `*` in submissions and console entries; a submission naming a real `x.discord.gg` is refused as another transform's host. Failure rows: one `finish` per exit, a Worker's own row not doubled, a `run`'s failure row survives `discard` (which needs an empty `result`, and then there is no error). SSE: `flush` and the subscription are one synchronous step, the cursor is `Last-Event-ID`, then `after`; named events carry no id; a reconnect re-renders. Slack's timer: a healthy socket gets `disconnect` long before 1.5 × life; at the moment it fires unacked events fail their ack and Slack redelivers to the next socket, deduped on `event_id`.
- Nits, not changed: a tab opened in the background holds a stream until first shown and hidden; `EventSource` gives up on a non-200 (a remote session expired), leaving "reconnecting…" until a reload; a `pull_request` action without a diff drops `files` from the frontmatter (the file stays). `X-HTTP-Method-Override` and `_method` are not stripped: none of the six services documents them; add the header to `HOP` if one does.
## Review round 11 — deadlines on a busy machine

Every timer in production code (`src/`, `src/console/static/app.js`, `scripts/`, and the one in `@hortus/core`), each asked: what fires it, can a full machine fire it falsely, what does that cost. Load below is `stress -c 24` and the subject pinned together to 4 cores, 6 runnable per core (so the soak beside it was left alone).

- **A submission's native call held hortusd's whole loop** (major, daemon down; `submit.ts › validate`, found measuring the transform deadline). QuickJS polls the interrupt handler only between operations, every ~10,000 of them, and never inside a native call. `"a".repeat(1<<22).indexOf("a".repeat(1<<12)+"b")` is 9.6 s in one call (n × m), a 64 KiB needle ran past 60 s, a 1 KB needle in `while (true)` past 60 s; a needle near the 32 MiB limit is hours. `transforms.submit` ran the tests on hortusd's thread with the Worker's code, role `worker`, no owner involved, so one RPC stopped the proxy, the console and every session, and SIGTERM with them (the handler needs the loop). Neither the old 100 ms clock nor round 10's was a bound here, and `TESTS_MS` was checked only between tests. Now `submit.ts › inThread`: the tests run in a `worker_threads` Worker (no dependency), this file its entry, terminated at `TESTS.ms`; a terminate does end a wasm call (measured: `process.exit` with a held call takes no time). One submission at a time, a second is told to retry, so a Worker cannot stack cores. Test: `submit.test.ts` "a submission's native call holds no loop" (the loop never stalls across a call that is minutes).
- **`TESTS_MS` 500 → `TESTS.ms` 30 s, and the total no longer judges** (minor): off hortusd's thread the bound guards one core, not the loop, so it is sized for a busy machine; a transform spending 90 ms on each of 32 calls now passes (the test pins this; it failed `over 500 ms`). Honestly: on HEAD an honest 32-test submission took 89–265 ms at this load, 20 of 20 passing, so the false firing was a margin of 2–5×, not an observed failure; the change comes with the thread. A submission's cost now: 130 ms idle, 800 ms at this load, thread start included.
- **The transform deadline counts the thread** (`transforms.ts › invoke`): on a test thread the process's CPU would be hortusd's loop too. `process.threadCpuUsage` where Node has it (22.23 here; `engines` says 22.15), else `cpuUsage` as before, and the clock stays for that case.
- **The new worst case of `invoke`, measured.** A polled spin: 103 ms idle, 604 ms median and 807 ms max at this load: 100 ms of CPU at the share the machine gives, the same work as the old bound, longer only by what the scheduler takes (old: 100 ms of wall time, which was the bug). Blocking without burning CPU, the way past a CPU rule, does not exist in here: `Atomics` is not defined, and QuickJS has no timers or I/O. Honest calls: 2,706 at this load with the libuv pool hashing and GC churn beside them, none interrupted, slowest 31 ms; a cold process's first call 30–47 ms wall, 7–13 ms CPU, 15 of 15 cold starts clean.
- **Ceiling: an approved transform on the request path.** `transforms.request`, `response` and `render` still run on hortusd's thread, synchronously, inside the pipeline, so an unpolled native call there holds the loop like the above. It needs the owner to have approved that code, and the shipped ones hold no hostile-sized needle (`indexOf`, `includes` on constants; regexes are polled; a linear `JSON.parse` of 16 MB is round 5's 180 ms). Upgrade: an async pipeline step on a pool of threads. That is a new subsystem, so it stays here.

| Timer | Where | Guards | False fire on a busy machine | Harm if so | Action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `DEADLINE_MS` 100 ms | `transforms.ts › invoke` | a transform's loop | no: needs CPU; 2,706 calls, 0 | 502 | thread CPU |
| `TESTS_MS` 500 ms | `submit.ts` | a submission's tests | margin 2–5× | a failed submission | 30 s, own thread |
| `boot_timeout_s` 120 s | `vm.ts › startVm` | a guest that hangs | not at this load: boot 1–3 s with KVM (design 13), 40× margin, by share ~6× slower; not run here | task failed, DM | none; a config key for a slower machine |
| `until` 5 s | `vm.ts`, virtiofsd sockets | a virtiofsd that never listens | no: 7 sockets 20–44 ms at this load; counted in sleeps, so load lengthens it | failed start | none |
| hours `deadline` | `vm.ts`, `dispatcher.ts` | the budget | it is wall time by v6 | budget is what it says | none |
| `killPath` 100 × 50 ms | `cgroup.ts` | a cgroup that will not empty | no: 201 processes, ~10 GB: 51 ms idle and at this load | a session's `wait` rejects | none |
| recovery 200 × 50 ms | `recovery.ts` | orphans dying | same; it only stops waiting | none | none |
