## Review round 11 — stop and reload

Walked every `stop()`, every `reload` (only the gateway has one) and every timer and loop in production code for an await that misses its abort, a `close()` waiting on a client that never leaves, a loop that re-arms. `src/e2e/stop.test.ts` runs the bar on a real daemon: Slack, Discord and Telegram live, an SSE client, a running session, a login view stalled in its navigation, a pending approval, a silent TCP client on each listener. A Settings save changing every connector: 6 ms (gateway 3 ms); with a reconcile stalled on Slack, 3,005 ms (the grace below). SIGTERM: 49 ms, exit 0, nothing left (an earlier run by module: browser 20, dispatcher 10, gateway 1, the rest 0). An early version of the test on the round 10 tree ran into `STOP_MS` at `console`: 60 s, exit 1. With only the login view's fix reverted: 60,008 ms, exit 1.

- **A silent client held every stop to `STOP_MS`** (major; `proxy/server.ts › unlisten`, `console/remote.ts › stopRemote`): `closeAllConnections` knows only sockets that reached HTTP: one in its TLS handshake and an upgraded one are not in its list, and `close`'s callback waits for them. Measured: a bare TCP connect to the phone's TLS port, 60 s and exit 1. A VM's WebSocket through the shared listener with QEMU killed (no FIN) is the same on the proxy. Neither waits for the callback now: the port is free when `close` returns; what is left ends with its client, `handshakeTimeout` or hortusd. Tests: `ws.test` "unlisten waits for no client", `remote.test`; both fail without it.
- **A first backfill held a stop and a Settings save for its whole length** (major; `gateway.ts › reconcileOnce`): `stopOne` awaits the runner and the runner awaits `reconcile`, and neither `history` nor `scopes` took the signal, so the save hung until the backfill ended (hours on a big workspace; `systemctl stop` until `STOP_MS`). It stops between batches and scopes; the cursor is the last batch's (`gateway.test`, fails without it).
- **Upstream calls had no abort at all** (major, same place): a stalled mirror or reconcile call waited out `IDLE_MS` (10 min), a 429 its `Retry-After`, five times over. `Runner.calls` is the runner's fetch signal, aborted `timing.graceMs` (3 s) after the stop, and `ConnectorDeps.signal` ends the connectors' own waits (`sleep` ×3). Not at once: a mirror in flight mostly finishes, because a Telegram message has no second chance. Queued events are drained for the same 3 s, then left: not inserted, not acked, so Slack and Telegram send them again. Ceiling: Discord's queue past 3 s never wakes the Planner (reconcile still mirrors the messages), the same as a message sent while hortusd is down. After a stop `calls` always aborts, so a webhook chore's pass or an owner's resync on the old connector ends too (`stopOne` waits for neither). Tests: "a stop cuts what its grace does not finish", all three in one stop, on stub connectors and the real Slack one; `stop.test` for a stalled reconcile through the real proxy. `gw.reload` stops its runners together. `coreFetch` keeps the signal off its `Request`, which holds a listener on it until collected.
- **A session starting at the signal outlived the stop** (major; `dispatcher.ts › start`, `runPlanner`): `stop` killed `r.child`, unset until `startVm` or `spawnSandboxed` returned, then awaited a session nobody would kill: `STOP_MS`, whenever a stop met a start. The `closing` check after the spawn already existed; `stopping` joins it. Tests: `dispatcher.test` "a stop while a VM is starting", "a stop while a host session is starting" (each fails without it). `stop.test` opens a session at the signal too, but it passed with the fix reverted: its timing does not reach the window. `runPlanner` has the same line and no test of its own.
- **`host.ts › kill` could miss a fresh session** (major, read and shown with a fake shim, not timed on the real one; the check above and `closing`'s both kill right after the spawn): the shim joins its cgroup itself after its exec, and `kill` only wrote `cgroup.kill`, so a kill that wins that race finds an empty cgroup and the session runs on: a close during a start waits on `hooks.kill`'s `r.done` for a Worker nobody killed. It SIGKILLs the child too. Test: the host test above, with a shim that never joins (fails without it).
- **A login view stalled in `Page.navigate` held `close`** (major; `login-view.ts › start`; round 10's nit, re-graded): `close` waits for the start, the start for the navigation, and that for the upstream, so a site that never answers held Reject for 10 min and a stop to `STOP_MS`. The navigation is not awaited (it rejects only on a dead browser, which `cdp.closed` handles); Chromium dying mid-navigation now reads `closed: the browser exited`, not `error`. `stop` also waits for a view still launching (bounded by `connect`'s 15 s), which round 10 left to the unit's kill. Test: `stop.test`.

What each stop does, measured on that run or read:
- `browser`: closes views and starts (20 ms; a launch ≤ 15 s). `planner`, `schedules`, `vault`: clear a timer; `tick` is synchronous, `wake` is guarded by `ctx`. `dispatcher`: 10 ms. `console`, `webhooks`: sockets dropped, 0 ms; `poll` and `redeliver` are not waited for. `gateway`: 1 ms idle, ≤ 3 s with anything in flight. `proxy`: 0 ms. `mirror`, `runtime`, `transforms`: nothing. Loops that could re-arm: `eventsLoop`, `reconcileLoop`, the factory retry and every connector's backoff sleep on the signal; `coalesce`'s window is unref'd and its one late DM is refused and logged.
- Left, nits: a stop still waits for a host start in `prepareClone`, local git only, so a clone of many GB can reach `STOP_MS`; a tab opening a login view between `browser` and `console` stopping starts a Chromium that dies with the unit; a reload in flight at the stop may start a runner that ends at `process.exit`; two saves at once overlap the old and new runner for one stop's length. An upgraded socket from a VM killed mid-WebSocket stays open while hortusd runs (no FIN, and a quiet WebSocket has no idle cut): one fd and one upstream connection per such kill.

`STOP_MS` and exit 1: after these it needs a wedged cgroup or a giant clone. What a forced exit skips is the crash path: `-2` rows released by `createGateway`, tasks by `recover`, `hours` lost to at most 5 s, the WAL replayed. `systemctl stop` and `restart` do not auto-restart whatever the code (`Restart=on-failure` does not apply to a requested stop); the unit reads `failed` until the next start, which is true. Only a bare `kill` of a hung daemon restarts it, one start, far from `StartLimitBurst`. Nothing changed.

## Review round 11 — failures on a real guest

The image from the round 10 tree (agent code unchanged since 4e8c877, not rebuilt: nothing here changes it). The Worker is uid `agent` with no sudo, so `!bash` cannot reach `/proc/sysrq-trigger` at all: the panic runs from a root service in a copy of the image (`vm.test.ts › crashImage`, debugfs, no sudo). Each case in `src/e2e/vm.test.ts › crashes` (real guest, fake Slack for the DM, `HORTUS_NO_NET`): waiting, the card's error, exactly one DM, one negative-seq `result` row, nothing from the Worker, then SIGKILL hortusd and restart: same status and error, not started again, no second DM or row. The Planner's reading of the row is step4's.

| Crash | Before | Now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| kernel panic at run time | `running` until `hours` (4 h by default), holding its `max_concurrent` slot: the queue behind it stopped | 1.2 s to `waiting`, `guest died: …` and the panic's tail |
| panic at boot | boot timeout (30 s in the test) | 4.9 s, `guest died: …` |
| Worker `kill -9` in the guest | `guest died: …` | same, 0.2 s |
| QEMU SIGKILL from outside | a serial tail with no word of why | 0.1 s, `QEMU killed by SIGKILL …` |
| virtiofsd SIGKILL | `running` until `hours` | 0.1 s, `virtiofsd exited: …` |
| hang (QEMU SIGSTOP) | `running` until `hours` | same (the ceiling below); STOP ends it in 30 s, no error, no DM, no row |

- **A kernel panic held the task and its VM slot for its `hours`** (major, `vm.ts › qemuArgs`). Arch's `CONFIG_PANIC_TIMEOUT=0` spins; round 10's `out/ended` needs QEMU to exit. Measured: with `vm.max_concurrent: 1` the next two tasks never started. `panic=1` with the existing `reboot=t` and `-no-reboot` ends QEMU. The old boot test relied on panic=0: a bad initramfs now panics and exits (new test), a bad root filesystem hangs in the initramfs shell and is the boot timeout's case.
- **A dead virtiofsd held the task likewise** (major, `vm.ts › startVm`): QEMU stays up, the guest blocks on its share. A virtiofsd's exit ends the VM a second later unless QEMU has gone by then: they exit together on every shutdown, in either order (a first version named virtiofsd on a killed QEMU). A virtiofsd that dies before QEMU starts is QEMU's connect error, as before. `vm.test.ts` (default suite, fake) and the real case.
- **QEMU killed outside said nothing** (minor, same function): the card showed the serial tail. A signal exit now names itself; `finish` puts budget, STOP, close and shutdown first, so those still read as they did.
- **A hostile Worker could boot itself in a loop** (major, `dispatcher.ts › finish`; the fake guest in `dispatcher.test.ts` "VM failures"): exit 0 (`out/ended` is the guest's word) with its inbox undrained re-queued the task each time, each session a new `result`: a batch, a coalesced DM a minute, for all of `hours`, and the batches eat `daily_tokens`, so an injected Worker silences the Planner for the day. Round 10's "bounded by the messages" held only for a Worker that drains. Now a clean exit re-queues only for a message that arrived during that session (`inbox.created > tasks.started`): an honest Worker drains at its start, so nothing older can be left (a message in the same ms is before the spawn, and drained; `>=` re-queued one test run in two).
- **`out/ended`, otherwise**: the guest writes it, so it can only lie about its own session: `0` hides its own crash from the DM (it could as well say `done`). A directory, link or FIFO reads as none (`readCapped`), so "guest died"; removed before each boot; junk is `Number(...) || 0`, a clean exit. It reaches no shell, path or SQL. `1e999` is a code of `Infinity` in a log line.
- **Flapping**: nothing else restarts a failed task but the owner's message: a crash waits, `restarts` lets nothing else through, a restart of hortusd leaves it waiting. So a task that panics on every start costs one row and one DM (at most one a minute per daemon life: the window is in memory, so a restart lets one through early) per owner message.
- **A guest that hangs without panicking runs until `hours`** (ceiling, decided): nothing distinguishes it from a long quiet tool, the only signal would be a guest heartbeat, a new mechanism, and a wall-clock one that fires when the machine is busy, killing a healthy session. Softlockup and hung-task panics were not turned on for the same reason (120 s in D on a loaded host is not death). It needs a stuck kernel or a frozen QEMU now a panic and a lost share end the VM. The card says `running`, STOP kills it in 30 s, the Planner's own VM stops at 0.5 h. Upgrade: `out/booted` touched by the guest, mtime read by `startVm`'s existing timer, with a limit in minutes.
- Nits, not changed: the card's tail for a Worker killed in the guest is systemd's terminal probe, not a reason (the first line has it, `console.log` the rest); the 1,500-byte tail cuts the panic's first line off behind the stack; on the card, tail lines hold escape sequences as text (HTML-escaped; the CLI's `s()` strips).

## Review round 11 — the soak and the runs

- **The hour's soak** (`HORTUS_TEST_SOAK=1`, 60 min, on `45890bf`, beside the forks' suites): 37,545 tasks; after warm-up (15 → 60 min, 9,355 → 37,545 tasks) rss +10.8 MB, **0.04 MB/100 tasks** (round 7: 0.02), heap −4.9 MB, external 0, fds +1. Rss climbs 200 → 292 MB in the first 20 minutes with the heap flat, then holds at 294–308. Round 10's unexplained 15 MB step recurred and is explained: at 56.1 min rss 315 MB with heap 111 MB, at 57.1 min rss 300 with heap 49: a heap peak before a major GC, not native growth. The merged tree adds a signal to every connector call (`Runner.calls`): `bound` removes its listener at `close`, undici's `Request` drops its own by finalizer; read, not soaked.
- **`dos` "20 Workers" `ETIMEDOUT`: unknown, stays open.** Seen in rounds 6, 7 and twice in this round (24–26 of 1,280 searches, load average 12–19 from the soak and the forks). `syscall: read` means the kernel ended the socket; 60 s is far short of `tcp_retries2`, which leaves keepalive: Node's agent sets 1 s idle, libuv 1 s × 10 probes, so ten unanswered probes in ~11 s would do it. Not proved. Measured on this tree: ~165,000 keepalive probes per `dos` run, and loopback `softnet_stat` drops of 11,000–22,000 in some runs (the per-CPU backlog), so a lost probe is real, but the same runs show `TCPAbortOnTimeout` 0. Not reproduced: 2 suite runs at 64 beside `stress -c 32` clean; 4 `dos` runs beside `stress -c 96` failed the two flood tests by `gave up` at their own 60 s and 15 s bounds (3× oversubscribed), with no `ETIMEDOUT`. To settle it, read `TCPAbortOnTimeout` across a run that fails. Worst case by what was seen: a flooding Worker's own search errors, on loopback, and it can retry.
- **The baseline's two Landlock failures from the scratchpad tree are `/tmp`'s**: the unattended profile grants `/tmp` as `rn` (no execute), so a checkout under `/tmp/claude-1000/…` cannot run there. By reading, not rerun; an installed copy is not under `/tmp`.
- **Final runs, on the merged tree** (product code `48a5bc5`; only Markdown after it). `pnpm lint` clean. Default, `KEEP` unset: 3 × (568 tests: 539 pass, 0 fail, 29 skipped). `--test-concurrency=64` beside `stress -c 32`: 2 × 539/0. `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1`: 540/0. `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1`: 546 pass, **1 fail**: `planner.live.test` gave a DOF to "reply to bob", which a model can argue needs the channel; the prompt did not change this round, and alone the file passed 4 of 4, so a model's judgement at about 1 in 5, not a code defect. VM (no image rebuild: nothing the guest runs changed): 21/0, 3 skipped; with NET 12/0, 12 skipped.
- Still pending after round 11, all on the owner's credentials or hardware: real-service verification of all six connectors and a real phone passkey (the first-run list of round 10 stands). The hour's soak has run on `45890bf`, not on the merged tree. Open with no owner action: the `dos` `ETIMEDOUT` above.

## Review round 12 — a second reader for round 11

Read whole: `gateway.ts`, `submit.ts › inThread`, `pipeline.ts › routed` and `prepare`, `dispatcher.ts › finish`, `vm.ts › startVm`, the six manifests and connectors' `events`.

- **Discord's queue past the grace never woke the Planner** (minor, wrong result; `gateway.ts › eventsLoop`): the `break` left queued events for a redelivery Discord does not make, and the owner's message can be the one queued. Only a connector without `history` (Telegram) breaks now: its update comes again whole. The others' events go in; `mirror` fails at once on the cut signal, the row goes to the Planner, reconcile heals the mirror (`gateway.test` "past the grace", fails without it). A message Discord sends while no socket is open still reaches the mirror only: v6 line 126 says so, and resuming a session across a restart is a new mechanism. Ceiling, with its upgrade: `session` in `syncs`.
- **A clean guest could be failed by the virtiofsd watch** (minor, wrong result; `vm.ts › startVm`): a virtiofsd can hear QEMU close its socket before QEMU itself exits, and a QEMU over a second behind (freeing a big `vm.mem_mb` on a busy machine) was killed: `exit` SIGKILL, "the guest lost a share", a good run marked failed. Shown with a fake QEMU; the gap on a real guest is not measured. Past `out/ended` the watch does nothing. Test: `vm.test` "a clean end" (fails without it); a hostile guest gains nothing, `hours` still ends it.
- **Held.** `Runner.calls`: a mirror cut half way commits nothing (changes commit only on return), the row is released and wakes; the listener goes with the upstream request (`bound`) or never exists (a refused request), only Telegram's poll uses `AbortSignal.any`; each runner has its own controller, so a reload of one DOF cuts no other (and `stopOne` cuts only what it stops). Telegram's offset is memory only until the next `getUpdates`, so an update past the grace is replayed; a cut download committed the message without its file (round 13: the update is left for redelivery). `inThread`: 80 ms a submission (thread, QuickJS, tests); a top-level throw, a syntax error, `process.exit` (no `process`), an OOM and a loop all read `tests failed: tests/a.json: …` to the Worker, and no entry reaches Approvals; 32 MiB is the bound, `InternalError: out of memory`, and the thread is terminated in every path; after `validate` the main thread touches only size-bounded JSON and `diff`'s 1e6 cells. `created > started`: `now()` has milliseconds, and a message of the same millisecond or before the first drain is drained at the Worker's start, so nothing is lost; a task not re-queued is `waiting` with its message readable at the next start. `panic=1`: as round 11 measured.
- **Nits, not changed**: a backward clock step during a session can leave a message undrained on `waiting` until the next one (a `seq` read at start would remove it; chrony slews); a virtiofsd dying between the socket check and the watch is ms wide and the boot timeout covers it; a transform that echoes 25 MB fails with a 25 MB `diff` in the RPC error, to the Worker that sent it, one at a time; `inThread` has no `exit` listener, and a thread that exits silently (no path found) would read "ran over".
- **Documented, not fixed; fixed in round 13** (minor): a Telegram file download over 3 s at a stop or a reload is cut: the message stays, its `files/` entry has no file, and Telegram has no history to heal it. The same as a failed download; `file_id` is in the event row. Upgrade: a re-download pass over `events`, or a grace for downloads.

## Review round 12 — other spellings of denied calls

From the published docs and each router's source (`tdlib/telegram-bot-api` `HttpConnection.cpp`, `Query.cpp`; `rack` 2.2 `method_override.rb`; GitLab `config/application.rb`, `lib/api/api.rb`, `Gemfile`; `grape` `path.rb`). The rule that holds it together: `scope` is an allowlist on the forges, so what a pattern does not know is refused, not passed; only a spelling that stays inside `{project}/**` needs `consequential` to see it.

- **Method override reached GitLab** (major, security boundary; `pipeline.ts › prepare`). Round 11's nit, re-graded: GitLab's `application.rb` leaves `Rack::MethodOverride` in Rails' default stack, and `api/v4` is mounted behind it, so `POST …/merge_requests/1/merge` with `X-HTTP-Method-Override: PUT`, or `_method=PUT` in a form body, runs the PUT: a merge as an ordinary POST the `auto` judge may allow. Refused for any credentialed request: the header and its kin, `_method` in the query or a non-JSON body (encoded too; Rack reads a body with no content type as a form). Refused, not stripped, so nothing runs as a method nobody asked for, and the owner's card cannot read POST for a PUT. ponytail: a custom transform for an API that needs the header cannot use it through the proxy.
- **`.json` on GitLab** (major, security boundary; `routed`). `format :json` with one content type makes Grape route `(.json)`: `PUT …/merge.json` is the merge, in scope, not a pattern. Dropped for the match.
- **`routed` left gaps** (minor): a `/` it could only see after decoding (`…/merge%2f`, `…%2f/bulk-delete`) was neither trailing nor doubled when the patterns ran; `;x=1` and `\` stood as they came, and the dot-segment check did not know `..;`. No shipped service is known to route those; both are now one normalisation, `routed`, and the check reads its result, double encoding included (`%252e`; twice, not in a loop: 8 k nested `%25` would hold the thread). The request goes upstream as sent, as before: `path` is passed through untouched, nothing re-parses it on the way.
- **A body could hide behind a content type** (major, security boundary; `blind`): any `application/x-git-*` was exempt, so `POST …/issues/1/comments` with `Content-Type: application/x-git-x` left its body out of the hash and the judge's view: GitHub reads the JSON regardless. The exemption is git's own type on git's own path.
- **A push could show no refs** (minor, v6 "each push shows `old new refname`"; `prepare`): a gzipped body (`git http-backend` inflates it) or a command list over 64 KB gave a card with nothing on it and a hash with nothing in it. Refused; git sends neither.
- **Holes in the lists** (minor, v6 "every forge manifest marks … repository creation"; v7 "every REST door to a ref"): GitHub's `forks`, `generate`, `transfer`, `branches/**/rename`, `releases` (creates a tag), `PATCH repos/*/*`; GitLab's `releases`; Telegram's `kickChatMember` (still works since Bot API 5.3), `ban*`, `remove*`; Slack's `*.remove*`; Discord's `bulk*`. Duplicates deleted. Tests: `forges.test` `writes`, `reachless`, method overrides; `ws.test`.

| Service | Other way in | State |
| --- | --- | --- |
| GitHub | `/graphql` (`mergePullRequest`, `deleteRepository`) | no scope pattern: 403 for a task (tested); `gh` read queries fail with it, and a Worker has `curl` on REST (superseded by round 14: reads pass, mutations are consequential) |
| GitHub | `/repositories/<id>/…` | same; `DELETE` asks (`DELETE {api}/**`) |
| GitHub | `uploads.github.com` | a credential host with no scope pattern: 403 |
| GitHub | `github.com/api/v3`, web routes | no scope pattern, or no token auth upstream |
| GitHub | git over HTTPS | `POST {host}/**/git-receive-pack`, consequential; a delete or force shows `old → new` (0000000 for a delete) |
| GitHub | method override | refused; GitHub documents none |
| GitLab | `/api/graphql` | no scope pattern: 403 (tested) |
| GitLab | numeric id for `g%2Fp` | consequential patterns take `*`; an ordinary call matches no `{project}`: 403 (tested) |
| GitLab | `.json` suffix, `X-HTTP-Method-Override`, `_method` | fixed above (reachable) |
| GitLab | `%252F`, `/api/v3` | not `{project}`, 403; v3 is gone |
| GitLab | git over HTTPS | consequential |
| Bitbucket | `{uuid}` workspace or repo | consequential take `*`; else 403 (tested) |
| Bitbucket | `bitbucket.org/!api/…`, `/internal/…`, `1.0` | no scope pattern: 403 |
| Bitbucket | git over HTTPS | consequential |
| Slack | a workspace host, `edgeapi` | not a credential host: placeholder unmapped, 403 |
| Slack | method in the body or query | none: the method is the path |
| Discord | `/api/v10` or unversioned | `discord.com/api/**` takes both (tested) |
| Discord | `discordapp.com`, `ptb.`, `canary.` | not a credential host: 403 |
| Discord | gateway ops | nothing deletes over the gateway |
| Telegram | `method` argument, GET, case | round 11 |
| Telegram | `/bot<t>/test/<method>` | the test environment, where a production token does not exist; nit |
| all | batch endpoints | none on these APIs |

Nit: Git LFS (`{project}.git/info/lfs/**`) is in scope and ordinary: it writes objects and locks, no ref. Unverified against the real services, like the rest of this file's connector claims; GitLab.com's edge could drop the header, which only makes the refusal unnecessary there.

## Review round 12 — the soak and the runs

- **The hour's soak, alone** (`HORTUS_TEST_SOAK=1`, 60 min, on `fa2fbef`, the tree with `Runner.calls`): 40,480 tasks; after warm-up (15 → 60 min, 10,185 → 40,480 tasks) rss +83 MB, **0.27 MB/100 tasks** against the bound of 1, heap +17 MB (the last sample's point in the GC cycle), external +0.1, fds −3. That figure is mostly one step: rss 233 → 253 MB over 15 → 45 min (0.10), 253 → 312 between the 45 and 46 min samples with heap and external flat, then 311 → 316 to the end (0.06). The merged tree, 47 min beside 27 `dos` runs and `stress -c 12`: 26,795 tasks, 0.24 MB/100 tasks, the same step earlier (248 → 294 MB between 12 and 20 min), then 294 → 297 (0.02). Rss settles at about 300 in every run, as round 11's did (294–308), so a one-time native high-water mark, not `Runner.calls` and not a leak; what allocates it is not identified. A third run (23 min, merged tree with the test client below): 0.36 over a window that still holds the step.
- **`dos` "20 Workers" `ETIMEDOUT`: caught and closed. The test's own client.** `dos` beside the soak and `stress -c 12` (load 16), `TcpExt` and `softnet_stat` around each run: 27 runs in 45 min, **7 failed**, by 3–19 searches `ETIMEDOUT read`. `TCPAbortOnTimeout` equals that count run by run (4, 3, 3, 10, 19, 17, 4) and was 0 in all 20 passing runs; `TCPTimeouts` 0 in 6 of the 7 and `ListenDrops`, `ListenOverflows`, `TCPBacklogDrop` 0, so no retransmission gave up and no queue refused: the keepalive timer. `softnet_stat` drops were 27,752–28,716 in the failing runs, 0–27,652 in the passing. The cause: `hostile.ts › get` used Node's global agent, which sets `SO_KEEPALIVE` at 1 s, and libuv's 10 probes end a socket after ~11 s unanswered; 2,640 sockets waiting on a flooded daemon were 168,000 probes a run, which overflowed the per-CPU loopback backlog (`netdev_max_backlog` 1000, softirqs behind a loaded CPU) and dropped each other's probes. Ten in a row on one socket is the failure. `agent: false` in the test client: 12 runs under the same load, 0 failed, softnet drops 0 in every run, `TCPAbortOnTimeout` 0, probes 75,000 (at 26% a run, 12 clean by chance is under 3%). No production client does this: `hortusd` sets no keepalive on what it accepts, and the Worker's `fetch` (undici) probes after 60 s. The 75,000 left are `pipeline.ts › agents`' upstream sockets, keepalive at the same 1 s: a network silent for 11 s ends an upstream call that was waiting, which reads as a 502 and is retried. Nit, left. Round 11's worst case stands withdrawn: nothing a real Worker sees.
- **The baseline's Landlock failures from a `/tmp` tree** again decided where these ran: the frozen tree for the soak and `dos` was a sibling of the checkout, removed after.
- **Final runs, on the final tree** (uncommitted at the time; only this Markdown changed after the build). `pnpm lint` clean. Default, `KEEP` unset: 3 × (570 tests: 541 pass, 0 fail, 29 skipped). `--test-concurrency=64` beside `stress -c 32`: 2 × 541/0. `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1`: 542/0. `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1`: 548 pass, **1 fail**: `planner.live.test` gave a DOF to "remind me tomorrow", so as to confirm in the thread, the judgement round 11 saw; alone the file passed 3 of 4, about its 1 in 5, and no prompt changed. VM (no image rebuild: only host-side `vm.ts` changed): 22/0, 3 skipped; with NET 13/0, 12 skipped.
- Still pending after round 12, all on the owner's credentials or hardware: real-service verification of all six connectors, including this round's GitLab findings (`X-HTTP-Method-Override`, `.json`), which are from the source, not from gitlab.com; a real phone passkey. Open with no owner action: nothing above nit. The native step in the soak's rss is unexplained and bounded.

## Review round 13 — false positives from the refusals

Each proved with real `git` (2.56) or a request through the proxy (`forges.test.ts`, `ws.test.ts`, `pipeline.test.ts`).
- **A push over `http.postBuffer` asked three times, twice blank** (major, misleading screen and the owner asked for nothing; `pipeline.ts › prepare`). Over 1 MiB git first posts a bare `0000` to see that auth holds, and that POST matched `git-receive-pack` like the push: a blank consequential entry, then the push's own, then a blank one again, as each probe is a new single-use request. Measured on the three forges: three approvals and four tries for one push, on every push over 1 MiB, since step 6. The probe has no command, so a receive-pack writes nothing: it passes without an entry, only on git's path and only as the whole body (`content-length: 4`).
- **`git push --tags` of about 700 refs was refused** (major, normal use refused; `peekCommands`). Round 12's "git sends neither" held for gzip (`remote-curl.c` sets `gzip_request` for fetch only) and not for the 64 KB list: 1,200 tags are 90 KB. The cap is `REQUEST_BUFFER`, 1 MB, about 10,000 refs; past it the refusal says to push fewer. ponytail: the whole list is one approval, and the card shows every line.
- **`_method` as text was refused** (minor, a Worker's post refused; `overrideField`, `hasKey`). `a%3B_method%3DPUT` decoded its `;` into a separator, a `text/plain` body starting `_method=` matched `^`, and a multipart file holding `name="_method"` matched anywhere. A key is now a key: split on `&` and `;`, decoded, compared; bodies only where Rack parses one (urlencoded, no type, multipart by its part headers). Raw `;` stays a separator, because Rack 2.2 reads it so, and clients encode it.
- **`acme%2Fservices` read as the services of `acme`** (minor, an ordinary task asks every time; `prepare › apart`). `POST /projects/g%2Fservices/issues` split to `/projects/g/services/issues` and matched `projects/*/services**`: every POST on a project called `…/hooks`, `…/services`, `…/runners` or `…/integrations` went to the owner, past the judge. An in-scope project's encoded name stays whole in `apart`; the check still splits anything else.
- **Round 12's `blind` missed receive-pack's own type** (major, security boundary; `prepare › blind`). With that type off git's path the peeked prefix made `body` non-null, so `POST …/issues` with `0000{…}` hashed and showed `0000` and sent the rest unseen. The `body === null` clause is gone: a streamed body is blind unless it is git's type on git's path.
- **Held, by request:** `git clone`, `--depth 1`, `fetch --deepen`, `ls-remote` (protocol v2), `clone --mirror`, `push --mirror`, a 50 MB pack (one-off, three forges), `http.postBuffer=1024`; model calls of `application/octet-stream`, `application/x-ndjson`, 2 MB; `.json`, `;`, `%2f` and dots in GitHub contents and branch URLs, GitLab project and file paths, Bitbucket `src`, Discord and Telegram paths; git-lfs's batch on all three forges. What git shows on any refusal is `The requested URL returned error: 403`; the prompt's rule covers it for approvals, and for a final refusal the Worker can repeat the call with `curl` to read the reason.
- **Ceiling, GitLab LFS objects** (superseded by round 14: the transform drops that token) (not installed here, from `lfs_api_controller.rb`): the batch answers with a fresh `Basic` LFS token, which the proxy then refuses as `raw credential: basic:password holds no placeholder`, so LFS objects on gitlab.com do not transfer. That token is GitLab's own, narrower than the owner's, and is not scrubbed. Upgrade: map it to a handle in the transform's `response()`. GitHub and Bitbucket hand out presigned URLs, which carry no credential. A streamed LFS upload to a credentialed host asks each time, as any blind body does.
- **Nit:** a dripped command list re-joins its buffer per chunk (a ponytail in `peekCommands`; each turn stays short). A lower-case `%2f` project spelling is refused by scope, as before.

## Review round 13 — the walk

Every console page and CLI command on a real hortusd (`HORTUS_NO_NET`, a fresh home, then a seeded one: every failure kind, every entry kind, 120 tasks, 1,304 chat rows, Discord's 4014 through the real relay, the `/events` stream across a daemon restart) in headless Chromium at 1280 and 390 px. No page overflowed at either width (1265/1280 is the scrollbar).

- **Every failure DM linked a 404** (major; `server.ts › routes`): `task <id> failed <url>/tasks`, but Tasks is `/` and only `/tasks/<id>` was routed, so the one link a failure gives the owner answered "No page at /tasks". `/tasks` is the Tasks page now, so DMs already sent work too.
- **`hortus approvals approve` said "approved #n" for a merge that did not land** (major; `vault/index.ts › approval.decide`, `merge.ts › onApproved`): a dirty checkout keeps the entry approved with a reason, which the page shows; the CLI printed success and a later `hortus approvals` showed `approved`. `approval.decide` returns `message: approved, not landed: <reason> (Retry is on the Approvals page)`; the CLI prints it, and the page's result line too. There is still no CLI retry: `merge.retry` stays a button.
- **`hortus approvals` left out what the page warns of** (major; `hortus.ts › approvals`): v7 makes a CLI decision a console decision, but a tainted entry's taint, a request's rendered line (a Bitbucket merge's "cannot pin") and whether `--grant` applies were page-only, and a merge row had no text at all. A pending row now prints them under it; `approval.list` adds `grantable`.
- **A message to a paused task said nothing** (minor; `chat.ts`, `task.ts`): `done`, then `queued` for good, the reason only on Tasks and Settings. `html.ts › pausedLine` is the one line on all three.
- **"nothing pending" under a badge of 11** (minor; `html.ts › empty`): `?skip=` pages every list of a page together, so `older` on Decided said "nothing pending" and "no open tasks" on lists that have rows. Past the first page only `more`'s "showing none of N" speaks.
- **A stream ended by a non-200 said "reconnecting…" for good** (minor; `app.js › connect`): that is final for an `EventSource`, and a revoked or swept remote session gets a 401: no updates and live-looking buttons. The page reloads, which shows the sign-in (a login page has no `app.js`, so no loop; a chat's draft is in `sessionStorage`). A daemon down is a network error: measured, `reconnecting…`, then clear and re-rendered 6 s after it is back, draft kept.
- **git hides a refusal, and the prompt said a 403 on a push is a wait** (major, leaves a task stuck; `prompt.ts`): measured on git 2.56. A `POST git-receive-pack` answered 403 prints `error: RPC failed; HTTP 403 curl 22 The requested URL returned error: 403`, `send-pack: unexpected disconnect…`, `fatal: the remote end hung up unexpectedly`, then `Everything up-to-date`, the same for `{"approval"}` and for `hortus: …`, with no body shown; a 403 on `GET info/refs` shows a `text/plain` body as `remote: hortus: …` and a JSON one as nothing. So a push refused at the POST (a ref list over the cap, 1 MB since this round) ended the Worker's turn waiting for an approval that does not exist. The prompt now says to look first (`GIT_TRACE_CURL=1 git push … 2>&1 | grep hortus:`, which prints a refusal's line and nothing for a wait) and that `Everything up-to-date` after the error is not a push, and the refusal says what to do. ponytail: it rests on the Worker running the line; a report-status body in a 200 would show as `remote:` by itself, but it is a new mechanism on the push path. The image needs rebuilding.

Read and left as they are: taint lines and task grants on Approvals and the card; `--grant` on an entry that cannot take one ("this entry cannot be approved with a task grant"); failure cards for crash, both `budget_exceeded` forms, boot timeout, a dead guest and a failed start, each with what starts it again; the chat's cut notice and link; Sources' 4014, missing credentials, failed mirrors and the Planner's unset lines; the fresh home's empty pages, and a first chat without a provider (`worker.provider and worker.model must be set (Settings)` in the stream); the Worker's `403 {"approval"}`, 429 and `rpc body over 1 MB`, and each round 10–12 refusal, which name the rule that refused.

Nits left: `session.md` of a task with no session is a blank tab; a send with the daemon down says the browser's "Failed to fetch" (beside `reconnecting…`, draft kept); a blind upload's entry shows `(streamed)` with no size or reason; `unmapped placeholder` does not name the placeholder; a WebSocket refused for its upgrade count gets an empty 429; the CLI has no `budget` or note, so restarting a failed non-chat task needs the card.

## Review round 13 — forge lists, the Telegram download, planner.live

Sources: `github/rest-api-description` (`api.github.com.json`), `api.bitbucket.org/swagger.json`, GitLab's `doc/api/openapi/openapi_v3.yaml`, all fetched 2026-11-05 and searched by path, summary and description; docs.slack.dev's method list; core.telegram.org/bots/api (its first 100,000 characters; the prefixes otherwise from memory).

- **Forge routes that create a repository or delete without the verb** (minor, v6 "every forge manifest marks …"). In scope and ordinary until now, consequential now: GitHub `POST repos/*/*/security-advisories/*/forks` (a temporary private fork is a repository), `PUT` and `PATCH repos/*/*/import` (the source import, closed on github.com but still published); GitLab `POST projects/*/import/git`, `jobs/*/erase` (the job's log and artifacts), `housekeeping` (`task: prune` drops unreachable objects), `ml/mlflow/**/delete` (runs and experiments). Slack's `*.bulkDelete*` (`admin.conversations`, `apps.datastore`) slipped `*.delete*`. Tests: `forges.test` `writes`, `ws.test` "the shipped delete patterns"; each fails with its lines reverted.
- **The scope refuses, so no line.** GitHub `POST user/codespaces/*/publish`; GitLab `POST projects/user/:id`, `projects/import`, `projects/remote-import`, `projects/remote-import-s3`, `import/github`, `import/bitbucket`, `import/bitbucket_server`, `bulk_imports`, `offline_imports`, `groups/:id/projects/:id`, `groups/*`: no project in the path, so a Worker gets `project scope` 403 (`forges.test` `reachless`), stronger than asking. Marking one consequential would turn a refusal into a question. Bitbucket: `PUT repositories/*/*`, `forks`, `src` (a commit that deletes files) were all held already; nothing to add.
- **Policy, not the list**: writes outside the three categories go to the judge under `auto`, by design. GitHub `stacks/*/unstack`, `PUT` lists under `branches/*/protection/restrictions`, `actions/runners/remove-token` (returns a token); GitLab `PUT`/`POST projects/*/mirror/pull` (a pull mirror can move branches, a door to a ref that is settings by another name), `fork/:id`, `restore`, `reset_*`, `repository_storage_moves`, `import_project_members`; Bitbucket `downloads` (a same-named upload replaces the file), `pipelines-config/runners` (the hyphen spelling the `pipelines_config**` line does not catch).
- **`gh release upload` of a text file was refused** (minor, stranded Worker; GitHub manifest): `credentials.hosts` names `uploads.github.com`, `scope` did not, so a small text asset read `project scope … outside the task's repos`, while a binary one (streamed, so consequential, so past the scope) asked. `uploads.github.com/repos/{project}/**` joins `scope`. ponytail: GitHub Enterprise's upload path is `{host}/api/uploads/…`, still refused; a line when someone runs it.
- **Reads the widened lists catch: none.** Slack: all 39 `.delete*`/`.remove*` methods and the two `bulkDelete` in the published list write. Telegram: `delete*`, `remove*`, `ban*`, `kick*` are all writes, and a `get…` name cannot start with them; a `/file/bot…/delete-ban-remove.json` is ordinary. Discord's and the forges' patterns name a write verb, so a `GET` passes them by construction. Tests: the "ordinary" half of `ws.test`. One POST that reads: GitLab's `repository/blobs/batch` matches `POST projects/*/repository/**`; patterns only add, enumerating that line's seven write routes risks a door for a rare call that has a `GET` twin and a clone beside it. A nit.
- **A Telegram file cut by a stop is no longer lost** (was Documented, round 12; major, data loss). The offset moves only at the next `getUpdates`, and a stop polls no more, so the update is still Telegram's. The connector rethrows a download failure once `deps.signal` is cut; the gateway, for a connector without history, deletes the row (nobody has seen it: it is held at -2, or a bot's -1) and the next start takes the update as new, whole, and wakes once. `gateway.test` "a Telegram file cut by a stop" runs the real connector on the fake; it fails without the rethrow. Ceilings: Telegram keeps an unconfirmed update for 24 h, so a daemon down longer loses it as it loses everything else; a SIGKILL mid-download leaves the row with nothing committed, so the replay is dropped as a duplicate: the row reaches the Planner, the message and its file never reach the mirror; a DOF removed by the save that cut it stays gone, as the owner asked.
- **`planner.live` is a tally** (test). One batch failed 1 run in 4 and meant nothing: the rate per decision is low, the batch holds five of them. It now runs 5 batches at once (47–60 s) and bounds the sum: at most 3 unneeded DOFs of ~25 and at most 1 needed DOF missed (never asserted before). Measured this round: 21 batches, 3 unneeded (all `slack` to "reply to bob"), 0 missed; so about 1 in 33, against round 8's 3 of 15 without the rule. At 1 in 25 the bound fails about 1 tally in 50; at 1 in 5 it passes about 1 in 4: it catches the rule's removal most of the time, not always, and more runs cost more than the catch is worth. The prompt is unchanged: "reply to bob" can be argued for and the prompt already says no, so a model that disobeys 1 time in 33 is not a wording defect; no image rebuild. 4 of the 42 needed DOFs came as `D1` or `slack:D1`: the real `task_create` answers `unknown DOF` and the model retries; the stand-in accepts them.

## Review round 14 — what one task leaves for another

Proved first through the real sandbox at `d56ac16` (`sandbox.test.ts`, core's real `bashTool` in a `spawnSandboxed` session): the overflow copy landed at `/tmp/hortus-bash-<uuid>.log`, mode 0644, under no task directory. This machine's `/tmp` is a 62 GB tmpfs.

- **A long output was a world-readable copy of what the task read** (major: a boundary and a leak; `host.ts › sandboxEnv`). A host session's `TMPDIR` is now `out/tmp`, made at spawn, in both profiles, and goes with `tasks/<id>` at close.
- **`/tmp` is read-only for unattended sessions** (major, a v6 change: v6 lists it as rw). `TMPDIR` alone leaves the Worker's own habit: `curl … > /tmp/resp.json` from two concurrent Workers overwrite each other (a wrong result, read back with no error), and `grep … > /tmp/hits.txt` crosses `scope.dofs` the same way the bash tool did. Delete `/tmp` from the grant and both end; the prompt says scratch goes in the work directory. **Ceiling:** a tool that hardcodes `/tmp` gets EACCES, loudly. `policy.cache: ["/tmp"]` gives the old grant back with the leak. Measured in a real session: heredocs, `mktemp`, `sort`, git (rebase, gc), Python `tempfile`, `multiprocessing` (Pool, Manager, forkserver, Lock), venv, pip, gcc, make, npm, pnpm, gh, go and a node unix socket all work; pnpm needed `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (`/tmp/pnpm-store-operation-locks-<uid>` otherwise, regardless of `TMPDIR`), set to `out/tmp`. tmux still fails (`/tmp/tmux-<uid>`, `TMUX_TMPDIR` would move it): it did before too, since `rn` forbids connecting to its own socket.
- **`~/.cache` and the other shared caches were the owner's** (major: a boundary; `landlock.ts › RW_HOME`, deleted). v7 said they "hold nothing the owner runs". On this machine `~/.cache` holds `ms-playwright` and `puppeteer` (browsers the owner's scripts execute), `~/.cargo/registry/src` (compiled, `build.rs` run, by the owner's `cargo build`, unverified once unpacked); elsewhere `pre-commit`'s hooks. A prompt-injected Worker rewrites one and the owner's next run executes it outside every sandbox, with `vault.json` in reach. The Workers' caches are now their own, `$HORTUS_HOME/cache` (`rn`; links, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, `GOMODCACHE`, `MAVEN_OPTS`): nothing under `$HOME` is writable. Cost: one cold start, and a tool that ignores XDG gets EACCES. `GOMODCACHE` also gives Go a module cache, as `~/go` is read-only. `caches()`'s "only an existing target" branch is gone with the owner's paths.
- **A Worker could read and write the owner's terminals** (major: a boundary; `landlock.ts › profile`). `/dev/pts` was `rn`, and its nodes are the owner's: `cat /dev/pts/3` steals what is typed there (a `sudo` password), and a write puts text on the owner's screen. TIOCSTI answered EIO here (`dev.tty.legacy_tiocsti = 0`), so no injection, on this kernel. Landlock cannot tell a session's pty from the owner's, so `/dev/pts` is unreachable for unattended sessions and `ptmx` left the rw list. **Ceiling:** no pty on a host Worker (`script`, `expect`, Python `pty`); a VM has its own. Chat is unchanged.
- **Chromium dies on a long `TMPDIR`** (found checking the fix; `cdp.ts › launch`). Its singleton socket goes under `TMPDIR`, and past 62 bytes of it (`HORTUS_HOME` over 31) `process_singleton_posix.cc` is a FATAL at start. The Worker's Chromium gets `TMPDIR=/dev/shm`, whatever the home. **Ceiling:** a killed browser leaves its empty `org.chromium.Chromium.*` directory there (a socket and a link, no content; 3,588 of them had piled up in `/tmp` the same way).
- **`sun_path` with a long `HORTUS_HOME`:** Python's `multiprocessing` adds about 33 bytes to `TMPDIR`, so `HORTUS_HOME` over 44 bytes (with a 16-byte task id) is `AF_UNIX path too long`; measured at 191 bytes of `TMPDIR`. `~/.hortus` is 24 here. Ceiling, not fixed: a shorter `TMPDIR` needs a directory no other session reads, which `out/` is and `/tmp/<id>` is not.

What outlives a session or crosses sessions, after this round:

| What | Outlives? | Crosses? | Grade and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `/tmp`, unattended | no longer | no longer (read-only) | fixed |
| `/tmp`, chat | yes, what the session puts there on purpose | readable by all | ceiling: chat is `rw` on `/` by design and the owner is in it; tool temp goes to `out/tmp` |
| `out/`, `clones/<task>` | until close | no | by design |
| `schedules/<id>` | between runs of one timer, until close | same timer only | by design |
| `/dev/shm` | yes | yes | ceiling: kept `rn`, as `multiprocessing.Lock`, `sem_open` and Chromium need it. Only a deliberate write lands there (nothing names it as a temp dir, but Chromium's socket). Nothing attributes a file to a task, so nothing sweeps it: a hostile Worker can hold RAM after it ends. A `size=` on the mount is the owner's lever |
| `$HORTUS_HOME/cache` | yes | yes | ceiling: the v6 shared cache, a channel between Workers. A tainted Worker can poison a package for a clean one, in its sandbox: bounded by that Worker's scope, consequential requests still reach the owner, but its routine ones pass `auto` with no taint. Upgrade: task-local caches (delete the links), at the price of every task downloading everything. Grows until the owner deletes it |
| `policy.cache` | yes | yes | the owner's own list |
| project branches `hortus/<task>` | yes | through the origin | by design (v6) |
| SysV shm, kernel keyrings | yes (measured: `ipcmk -M` in a session survived it) | yes | ceiling: Landlock does not cover them, no tool here uses them, only a deliberate hostile pair of Workers; memory is bounded by `kernel.shmall`. Closing them takes an IPC namespace in the shim, a new mechanism |
| TCP on `127.0.0.1` | no (cgroup kill) | live sessions, on purpose | ceiling: as above, deliberate pair only |
| ptrace, `/proc/<pid>/environ`, signals, abstract sockets | no | no (measured: EPERM, EACCES) | closed by the domain |
| VM guests | no | no | `-snapshot` root on the guest's own kernel: its `/tmp` and caches go with the VM (`vm.ts`); no `TMPDIR` is set in the guest |

- A deliberate channel between two sessions needs both compromised, and the first can already do with its own scope and gate what the second could. That is why `/dev/shm`, SysV and TCP are ceilings and the accidental paths (tool temp files, a Worker's `/tmp` habit) were fixed.

## Review round 14 — test quality

- **A crashpad handler held a test process for 21 minutes** (minor in production, a hang in the suite; `cdp.ts › launch`). Chromium's handler daemonizes (PPID 1) holding the browser's stderr, our pipe; two of them outlived a browser in a loaded `browser.test` run and the test process, with nothing else alive, never ended. `launch` now destroys the child's stdio a second after `exit`, which also ends a per-launch leak of its fds in hortusd. Tested with a fake browser whose daemon holds stderr (fails without the fix).
  - **How a handler survives is unproven:** SIGKILL at random points 5–1,220 ms into 192 starts beside `stress -c 32` left none. The survivors themselves are not fixed, only that they hold nothing.
  - **Crashpad cannot be stopped.** `--disable-crash-reporter`, `--disable-breakpad` and `CHROME_HEADLESS=1` all leave both handlers running (process list, a browser up); `--disable-crashpad-for-testing` stops them and crashes the network service ("FD ownership violation"). `BREAKPAD_DUMP_LOCATION` moves their database, which was the owner's real `~/.config/chromium/Crash Reports` outside Landlock (the login view, in hortusd): now `<profile>/crash`, removed with the profile, so a dump of a login page no longer lands in the owner's config.
  - **Production:** no stuck task. `hortus-agent` ends with `process.exit` and does not wait for the loop, and `spawnSandboxed`'s exit path `cgroup.kill`s the session, which holds the handlers though they are reparented. A login view with the network layer has its own cgroup, killed on release. Under `HORTUS_NO_NET=1` a login view has no cgroup, so a surviving handler stays until reboot, holding nothing (ceiling, dev only).

- **`load.test` bounds the median round** (`pct(p95, 0.5)`, three rounds): one saturated round still passes (595, then 210, 156, 170 ms), and a regression in two rounds of three fails. `Math.min` passed it.
- **`step4blive`'s failure message keeps the session's stdout.** Round 13's new prompt line cannot be the cause of "a thread channel": it is inside `# Forge repositories`, rendered only with `repo_urls`, and this task has none, so the prompt the model saw was unchanged by round 13.
- **"A thread channel" was a defect, not the model** (major: a wrong result in normal use; `planner.ts › renderEvent`). Eight runs with the session kept: 6 passed, 2 failed, and both failures posted `{"content":…,"message_reference":{"message_id":"3100"}}` to `/channels/3000/messages`: HTTP 200, done, in the parent channel. A brief showed only an event's first ref, and `discord:<parent>:<thread>` has the shape of a plain message's `discord:<channel>:<message>`; the transform's comment said so too. Round 13 graded this "model" and moved on. A brief now shows every ref, so a thread's `discord:<thread>:<thread>` is there, and the transform's comment says how each reads. The connector's second ref is keyed on the message's channel, so a forum post's first message (thread id = message id) has it too. After the fix: 8 of 8 runs, all three tests.


## Review round 14 — gh, GraphQL and auto

Real `gh` 2.102 through the real TLS listener against the fake GitHub, under `auto` with a fake judge (`src/proxy/gh.test.ts`, default suite, skipped without `/usr/bin/gh`). `gh` reaches the listener by CONNECT through a local forwarder (`HTTPS_PROXY`), so it speaks to `api.github.com` with the session's env and nothing can leave; the transparent path differs only below TLS, so `HORTUS_TEST_NET` was not needed.

- **`gh` did nothing but `gh api` on REST** (major, normal use refused with an unreadable reason; `transforms/github`, `pipeline.ts › prepare`). `GH_TOKEN` is set for Workers, and `gh pr view`, `gh pr list`, `gh pr create`, `gh issue comment`, `gh pr merge` and `gh repo clone` all open with a GraphQL call, which had no scope pattern: `HTTP 403: 403 Forbidden`, and the `hortus:` line is in a body gh does not print. **Decision: GraphQL passes, classified like REST.** Every method on `{api}/graphql` and `{host}/api/graphql` (Enterprise's real path) is consequential, except a request the transform vouches for with `read: true` on its template (`Template.read`, a new optional field, kept out of the hash) when the task has repos of that credential. A vouched read then goes through the gate as a routine request: the judge under `auto`, the owner under `human`, a tainted task to the owner.
- **The read rule has no parser.** `mutation` is a keyword, so in any write it is spelled literally in the document: GraphQL has no escapes outside strings, and JSON can only reach it as itself or `\\u` escapes. The rule: POST, no query string, no `content-encoding`, `application/json`, JSON with a string `query`, and `/mutation/i` nowhere in the whole body with `\\u` decoded. It holds whichever parser runs upstream (duplicate `query` keys, a batch array, a form-typed body and a document in the URL all stay consequential; tested). **Ceiling:** the word in a variable, a search string or a field alias asks the owner per request, the safe side; `subscription` is not checked (GitHub has none over HTTP, and it writes nothing).
- **Repos before a read** (`pipeline.ts › prepare`): GraphQL has no project in its path, so the token is what narrows it. A task with no repos of that credential would mint `repositories: []`, which GitHub does not document as "none", so its reads stay consequential. A public repository is readable through a narrowed token, as it is with no credential at all.
- **A wait through `gh` read as a bare 403** (major, leaves a task stuck: the `403 {"approval"}` rule was invisible; `pipeline.ts › pendingOut`): gh prints an error body's `message` and nothing else, so the JSON gained `message: "hortus: approval <id> is waiting for the owner"`, and `gh` shows `HTTP 403: hortus: approval 7 is waiting for the owner`. A plain-text refusal stays a bare `HTTP 403`; the prompt points at `GH_DEBUG=api` as it points at `GIT_TRACE_CURL` for git.
- **A PR opened by `gh pr create` had no ref** (major once gh works, a review or CI result never reached the task; `transform.js › response`): `createPullRequest` and `createIssue` now give refs from their `url`, like REST's `html_url`.
- **Neither the judge nor the owner saw the query string** (major, misleading screen; `pipeline.ts › summarize`): `path` was the path alone, so `GET /bot…/sendMessage?chat_id=1&text=…`, `POST chat.postMessage?channel=…&text=…` (both APIs read it) and any `?…` on a credentialed call showed only the method's name, while the hash covered the rest. The summary's `path` now carries the query.
- **A body past 2 KB hid its tail from the judge and the owner, with nothing saying so** (major, security boundary; `pipeline.ts › summarize`, `gate.ts › request`): the preview was a silent `slice(0, 2048)` of a body up to 1 MB, so 2 KB of padding in an issue's JSON put the real fields out of sight. The preview is now 8 KB (gh's largest read is 1.7 KB, so normal requests stay whole), past it the text says `… N more chars not shown` and `summary.cut` is set, and a cut request is never judged: it goes to the owner with the reason. **Ceiling:** the owner sees the first 8 KB and the marker, not the tail; a consequential request can be approved unread, so the marker is the signal to reject. The WebSocket frame preview (`ws.ts`) stays 2 KB: it never reaches a judge, and only `ws:text` manifests have frames with credentials.
- **Publishing a draft release** (minor; manifest): `PATCH repos/*/*/releases/*` makes the tag a `POST releases` would, and passed as routine (`gh release create` with assets ends with exactly that call). Consequential.
- **What a Worker should do** (prompt, `# Writing to sources`): REST for writes, as before, since each gh mutation goes to the owner where the REST call may be judged; `gh` for reads where it is installed (host sessions: the VM image has none, and `GH_TOKEN` is set only by `sandboxEnv`). The line is conditional on `scope.credentials` naming `github`, so the prompt stays a pure function of task.json.
- **Under `human`, a client of N gated requests costs more than N approvals** (ceiling, not changed): a request's approval is single use and each `gh` run starts again from its first request, while an approved entry waits for its next use. Measured with instant approvals: `gh pr view` and `gh issue comment` 4 runs, `gh pr merge` 5, `gh pr create` (4 gated requests) 8. It ends, but each run is a click; v6's `human` is per request, `auto` is the policy for a Worker using `gh`, and REST through `curl` is one request per step. Same for git's `GET info/refs` before a push.
- **Left as they are:** a GraphQL mutation names node ids (`pullRequestId: "PR_kw…"`), so the owner sees the operation and its variables but not the repository; the token is narrowed to the task's repos either way. `gh repo create` is a mutation with no `administration` token: approved, it fails upstream, and the Worker has REST's `POST /user/repos`. `GH_TOKEN` is set only for a credential named `github`, and `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` not at all, so gh on a DOF of another name or on Enterprise says it is not signed in, and the Worker has `curl`. gh's HTTP cache (`~/.cache/gh`, used for its schema probes and `gh api --cache`) is in the shared rw cache: angle 1's list. GitLab's `/api/graphql` stays refused (nothing in `glab`'s ordinary use was measured here).


## Review round 14 — LFS, uploads and package managers

- **The boundary cut only delimiter lines** (major, found merging: `pipeline.ts › hashTemplate`). The first cut split the body on the boundary anywhere, so an approved `boundary=XX` body with `pay XX` in it hashed the same as `boundary=evil` with `pay evil`: a different payload on one approval. The body now splits on `\r\n--<boundary>` only, which is framing to any parser; a bare-LF delimiter stays uncut and just asks again.

Real clients through the real proxy. `curl -F`, `npm`, `pnpm`, `pip` (bundled with `ensurepip`) and `uv` ran as installed; git-lfs 3.8.1 and glab 1.128.0 are official release binaries in a scratch directory, each matching its published sha256. git-lfs is Go and ignores `http.curloptResolve`, so it and glab ran in `unshare -rmn`: a private network namespace with its own `/etc/hosts`, the proxy on `127.0.0.6:443` and `sandboxEnv`'s production `insteadOf`, so nothing could reach a real forge. `src/proxy/clients.test.ts` keeps curl's flows in the default suite; the rest needs binaries the suite does not have, so they were one-off runs. cargo is not installed: its shape (parallel GETs of `application/octet-stream`, a 30 s no-progress timeout) was reproduced with `curl -Z`.

- **An approved `curl -F` never passed** (major, a task stuck; `hashTemplate`). curl, requests and most clients draw a new multipart boundary per run, and it sat in the hashed `content-type` and body, so "retry exactly the same request" was a new request: approve, retry, a new entry, for ever. Every Telegram `sendDocument` and Discord attachment hit this. The boundary is cut from both; the body hashes as the pieces between boundaries, so where each one stood still counts (`--a` as text in one body and a delimiter in another hash apart).
- **A slow download sent no byte until it was whole** (major, normal use fails; `respond`). Every response but SSE and git's was held in `hortusd` up to 16 MB or 30 s, headers included. Real pip on a 12 MB wheel at 500 KB/s: `Read timed out. (read timeout=15)`, every retry. Any link under about 8 Mbit/s fails on a large enough wheel, and cargo's 30 s timeout is next. It also took one of the client's 4 buffer slots and one of 16 for all Workers, so model calls queued behind slow downloads. A host with no transform has nothing to read in a body (`Set-Cookie` is captured before this point, and a site manifest makes a transform), so it streams from the first byte. So does `application/octet-stream` on a transform's host: `response()` has no use for one, and it covers GitLab's proxied LFS objects. Same pip run after: 25.5 s, the link's speed. Cost: such a response loses `content-length`, as one over 16 MB always did; a cut upstream is now a cut chunked body, not a 502. `dos.test`'s bodies moved to a host with a transform, so it still measures the slots.
- **`~/.config/glab-cli` was readable by every session** (major for an owner who uses glab, security boundary; `denySet`). Its `config.yml` holds the owner's token in plain text, like `~/.config/gh`, and could leave for any host in `origins`. Denied in both profiles; sessions get `GLAB_CONFIG_DIR` under `out/work/.cache`, as `GH_CONFIG_DIR`. Add it to the deny list under Security › Host.
- **A leading BOM was dropped from a text body** (minor, data; `utf8`). `TextDecoder` strips it by default and the decoded string went upstream: `curl --data-binary @windows.csv` arrived three bytes short. `ignoreBOM` keeps it.
- **GitLab LFS uploads** (round 13's ceiling, closed; `transforms/gitlab`). The published `lfs_api_controller.rb` confirms it: `lfs_auth_header` is a fresh `LfsToken` for any user (the echo of the request's header is only for a request with no user, so not for the connector's token), and on gitlab.com downloads are signed URLs, so only uploads were refused. Round 13's upgrade was a handle; smaller is to drop `authenticated` and the `Authorization` from each action of the batch. git-lfs then gets a 401 and sends its own credential, the placeholder. The Worker no longer holds that token either.
- **git-lfs's lock check doubled every push's approvals** (minor; `sandboxEnv`). `POST …/locks/verify` before each push is a credentialed POST with a single-use approval, so under `human` each try alternated it with the batch, and 5 tries did not get one object up. `lfs.locksverify=false`: a Worker takes no locks.
- **A reply to a document the Worker sent was not routed** (minor; telegram `response`). Refs came from `sendMessage` only; now any `send*` that answers a Message, in any case.

Held on all three forges, with real git-lfs, push then fetch then `fsck` (shapes from each service's published behaviour, unverified against them):

| Forge | Batch | Transfer | Asks under `human` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GitHub | `Basic` from `insteadOf` | signed S3 PUT, `RemoteAuth` verify on `lfs.github.com`, signed GET: nothing from the vault | one per batch |
| GitLab | the same | PUT on `gitlab.com` with the placeholder (blind, so the owner's); signed GET | batch and each object |
| Bitbucket | the same | `Bearer` on `api.media.atlassian.com`: the forge's own, nothing from the vault | one per batch |

Other clients: Telegram `sendDocument`, Discord `payload_json` plus `files[0]`, text and binary, and 5 MB with `Expect: 100-continue`: wait for the owner, pass on the retry, arrive whole. Slack: `getUploadURLExternal` and `completeUploadExternal` are gated; the upload to `files.slack.com` carries no credential and passes ungated, by multipart or raw, and nothing is shared until the gated complete. npm, 301 packages with a 60 MB tarball: 2.0 s, peak 15 connections. pnpm at `--network-concurrency=300`: 10.6 s, peak 32, no 429. `curl -Z` at 300: all 200 in 0.5 s. pip with only `sandboxEnv`'s CA variables, and uv: pass. glab: `api`, `issue list`, `mr list`, `issue note` and `mr view` pass with `PRIVATE-TOKEN` mapped, and the approval JSON shows in its error. So 256 in flight, the body budget and the turns do not hurt normal use.

Ceilings and nits, not changed:

- **git-lfs lists `transfers` in Go's map order**, so a retried batch can hash differently and ask again (seen: 2 orders, 1 to 3 asks per batch under `human`). It converges, since an approved order stays approved. Sorting it in three transforms for one policy's extra click is not worth it.
- **A refused or waiting git-lfs says `batch response: Authorization error`**, not git's 403, so the prompt's rule does not name it, and the approval JSON is hidden unless the Worker repeats the call with `curl`. git-lfs is not in the image and the prompt does not mention it; a host Worker with it installed reads a failure. Say so in the prompt if LFS repositories become routine.
- **`glab repo clone` and anything else calling `GET /user` are out of scope** (`hortus: project scope`, shown plainly); `/api/graphql` likewise, by round 12. `GITLAB_TOKEN` is not set, as the prompt steers to `git` and REST. Nothing consequential passes differently through glab.
- **A binary or over-1 MB upload is `(streamed)` on the card**: the owner sees method and path, not the caption or the chat of a `sendDocument`. As recorded for any blind body; the path says what it is.
- **With an `origins` list**, LFS and Slack uploads need their transfer hosts listed (`files.slack.com`, `lfs.github.com`, `github-cloud.s3.amazonaws.com`, `github-cloud.githubusercontent.com`, `media.githubusercontent.com`, `api.media.atlassian.com`, the store behind gitlab.com); the refusal names the host.
- **A file naming a placeholder or a handle** (`hortus-secret-x`, `{secret:x}`) is refused as a text upload and passes as a binary one: the pipeline's steps 5 and 7, not new.
- Not run: cargo, Maven, Gradle (no binaries here). Real services: as ever, pending.

## Review round 14 — the runs and what is left

- **Final runs,** all on `1006741` (the docs alone changed after it), image rebuilt on that tree. `pnpm lint` clean. Default, `KEEP` unset, 3 × (597 tests: 568 pass, 0 fail, 29 skipped); `--test-concurrency=64` beside `stress -c 32`, 2 × 568/0; `HORTUS_TEST_NET=1` 569/0; `HORTUS_TEST_LLM=1` 576/0; VM 22 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped; VM with NET 13 pass, 0 fail, 12 skipped. A per-file 20-minute bound ran beside them and never fired. An earlier loaded run hung on the crashpad handler (above) and does not count.
- Still pending, all on the owner's credentials or hardware: real-service verification of all six connectors, with this round's GraphQL shapes, LFS flows and upload flows; a real phone passkey.
- Decided (round 15; the owner can override either): **`$HORTUS_HOME/cache` stays shared.** A task-local cache makes every task download everything, and a poisoned package still runs inside that Worker's sandbox, bounded by its scope, with consequential requests still going to the owner: a recorded ceiling (table above). **`/tmp` stays read-only for unattended host sessions**; a project that needs it has `policy.cache` or a VM (round 15 › real use).
- cargo, Maven and Gradle were not exercised here; round 15 ran all three (below).

## Review round 15 — a second reader for round 14

- **A Worker could read the owner's keyboard and screen** (major: a boundary; `landlock.ts › profile`). Round 14 closed `/dev/pts` and left `ro` on the rest of `/dev`. Reading a device needs no ioctl, so that was enough: through the real shim, under the unattended profile, `/dev/input/event*` (the owner is in `input` here; a unit's `User=` brings the groups), `/dev/fb0` and the owner's console `/dev/tty2` all opened. An unattended session now has only `/dev/{null,zero,full,random,urandom,tty,shm}`: `/dev` is left out of the base grant, and round 14's walk of it and its link branch are gone. `/dev/stdout` and `/dev/fd` are links and need no rule. Checked with `hortusd`'s stdout on a file, a pipe, `/dev/null`, closed, closed stdin, and a user unit with journal and null stdio; the VM path has no Landlock. `policy.cache` can name a device.
- **Chat** (the question round 14 left): the same read, but not the same boundary, as a chat is `rw` on `/`, so a tainted one could already edit `~/.bashrc` and wait. Closing `/dev/pts` and `/dev/input` still takes the read of what is typed now, and costs little: `hortus chat` is pipes (`spawnSandboxed` has no pty in either kind), so only `script`, `expect` and Python `pty` go. Both are denied in every profile, which also deleted the `kind` branch. **Ceiling:** a chat keeps the rest of `/dev` (`/dev/fb0`, consoles, the owner's boards, `/dev/kvm`): the owner is in it, and a hardware project needs it.
- **A cookie the site set again reached the Worker with its real value** (major: a boundary; `pipeline.ts › store`; older than round 14, found testing its streaming). `store` replaced a row by delete then insert and trusted "the row id moves", but `captured.id` has no AUTOINCREMENT: deleting the newest row gives its id back, the scrubber's key (count, max id) stays, and the cached scrubber lacks the new value. So the real value went out in `Set-Cookie` and the body, on both the streamed and the buffered path, for any site that rotates its one session cookie. Now insert, then delete. Reproduced and tested on a real curl.
- **`hashTemplate`'s second version still let an approved body vouch for another** (major: a boundary; `pipeline.ts › hashTemplate`, `boundaryOf`). Three holes. A line that only starts like a delimiter was cut, so under `boundary=to=alice` the text `--to=alice 100` approved `--to=mallory 100`: the split now takes whole delimiter lines (`(--)?`, padding, CRLF or the end). A decoy (`x="boundary=a"; boundary=b`, `xboundary=a`, a second `boundary=`) made this code cut one string while the upstream parsed another: such a type is not cut, and `overrideField`, which the same decoy blinded, scans the whole body. And `boundary=\S+` also ate parameters after it. **Held:** a boundary inside a part, a preamble, an epilogue, padding, `filename`, and a quoted boundary. Real clients, each three runs into a capture server, one hash per client: `curl -F`, Python `requests` 2.34, Node 22's `FormData`, Go's `mime/multipart`. **Ceiling:** a boundary with a space in it is not cut, so it asks again per try; a parser that takes a line merely starting with the delimiter as one can frame an approved text differently (no parser in the shipped stacks was shown to).
- **`operationName` was not on the card** (minor: `transforms/github › render`): it picks which of a document's mutations runs, and sat in the folded body. Now `GraphQL: runs Evil: …`.
- **`go get`, `go install x@latest` failed** (major, normal use; `host.ts › caches`): `~/go/pkg/sumdb` and `~/go/bin` are read-only. `GOPATH` is `out/work/.cache/go`; `GOMODCACHE` stays shared.
- **The JVM ignores `TMPDIR`** (major, normal use; `caches`): `/tmp` is compiled in, so `createTempFile` and JUnit `@TempDir` threw under Maven and Gradle. `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=-Djava.io.tmpdir=<out/tmp>`, at one `Picked up…` stderr line per JVM. This was the only evidence that read-only `/tmp` breaks normal use, and this is its smallest fix: `/tmp` stays read-only.
- **`uvx`, `uv tool` failed** (major, normal use; `caches`): `XDG_DATA_HOME` is `out/work/.cache/share`. **`./mvnw` unpacks into `~/.m2/wrapper`**: `MAVEN_USER_HOME` (shared `m2` unattended, `out/work/.cache/.m2` in a chat); the before case was reasoned, not measured.
- **`~/.m2/settings.xml` holds server passwords and every session could read it** (major: a boundary; `denySet`, as glab in round 14). Denied with `settings-security.xml`. Maven dies on an unreadable settings file, so `MAVEN_OPTS` moves `-Duser.home` for its JVM (`-s` in `MAVEN_ARGS` would beat a project's own). This machine has no such file: the deny was measured through `policy.deny`. A chat's Maven has not finished a build through this path.
- **The prompt's `/tmp` line** (minor): a brief naming `/tmp/report.py` recovered 3 of 3 before, but in 10 to 16 calls spent diagnosing the sandbox. It now says a refused write is the sandbox: do not investigate, put the file in the work directory, say so. Live, `deepseek-v4.1-flash:1017`, 3 runs per brief, old then new: "a temp file" 3/3 in 2–3 calls, 3/3 in 2–3; a brief naming `/tmp/report.py` 3/3 in 10–16, 3/3 in 4–5 with no note to the card; a throwaway C program 3/3 in 4–5, 3/3 in 2–4. No run waited on the owner.

Real use, through `spawnSandboxed`, unattended: gcc, make, cmake (`-pipe`, `-flto`, ASan), shell temp idioms, git (hooks, `rebase -i`, autosquash, stash, gc, worktree, bisect, filter-branch), Python (venv, pytest, `pip install -e`, uv, sqlite temp store), Node (`npm test`, `npm ci`, npx, `--cpu-prof`, pnpm), Go (build, test, `-race`), Maven 3.10, Gradle 9.8 and cargo 1.90 (official release, sha256 verified; build, test, `cargo add`, `cargo install`) all pass after the fixes above. Those fetches went straight to public registries without credentials, not through the proxy.

Held on a second reading, unchanged: the GraphQL rule (aliases, fragments, a string or comment holding the word, which asks the owner; `operationName`, GET, batches, persisted-query shapes all stay consequential); streaming with no transform (a value split across chunks, gzip, `Set-Cookie`, the HTML taint, a body cut mid-way fails the client's read: all tested now); `pendingOut`'s `message`; a brief's refs (at most three on Discord, a commit's PRs on a forge); `cdp.ts`'s one second (60 launches, a third with the loop blocked past it, kept the last stderr line: stderr is read in the same poll as the exit).

Ceilings:

- **A body over 8 KB under `auto` is one click.** Model calls never reach the gate (step 9, whatever their size), and Telegram (4,096) and Discord (2,000) cannot pass 8 KB, so this is a long GitHub comment or a Slack post of that size; `day.test`'s bodies are all far below it, so its load is unchanged. A bigger preview multiplies every approval row and the judge's prompt. The entry says why it came.
- A hardcoded `/tmp` (`mktemp -p /tmp`, `flock /tmp/x`) gets EACCES, loudly. `touch` on a file already in `/tmp` succeeds: Landlock does not cover `utimes`.
- A Worker's Maven loses the owner's mirrors and proxies, as with `~/.npmrc`. `./gradlew` downloads its distribution per task, as only `caches` is linked. A JVM ignores `SSL_CERT_FILE`, so Maven and Gradle through the proxy need the hortus CA in the JVM's trust store (not exercised).
- Round 14's mutants: 14 of the reverted fixes survived their tests. 13 now fail under tightened ones; `/i` on `mutation` is equivalent (keywords are case-sensitive; it is only the safe side).
- **`soak`'s short bound read a garbage collection as a leak** (test; `soak.test.ts`): it failed 3 of 4 loaded runs here. It subtracted the heap's change across the window, and a GC just before the last sample made a flat rss (+0.8 MB) read as +37 MB/100 tasks, with `heapTotal` too. It now subtracts what V8's peak in the window explains; a native leak still fails it. Then 4 of 4 loaded runs passed.
